f_{b}^{\pm}:=\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}b^{\pm}\cdot N)+\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}([\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1},b^{\pm},N])+[\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}},b^{\pm}\cdot]\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}N
. The
L^{2}(\Sigma)
norms of the last two terms in
f_{b}^{\pm}
can be directly controlled via Kato-Ponce type inequality (Lemma
B.6
) and interpolations

\displaystyle\left|[\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1},b^{\pm},N]\right|_{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle~|b^{\pm}|_{s-\frac{3}{2}}|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}+|b^{\pm}|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-\frac{3}{2}}\lesssim\|\partial_{3}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-2}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}+\|b^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm}|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-\frac{3}{2}}

\displaystyle\left|[\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}},b^{\pm}\cdot]\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}N\right|_{0}\lesssim

\displaystyle~|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}b^{\pm}|_{L^{\infty}}|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-1}\lesssim\|b^{\pm}\|_{2.5,\pm}|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-1}

Then the regularity of the free interface is given by

|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}\leq P(|\bar{b}^{\pm}|_{L^{\infty}})|f_{b}^{\pm}|_{0}\leq P(\|b^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm},|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|b^{\pm}\|_{H_{*}^{s}(\Omega^{\pm})}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|b^{\pm}\|_{H_{*}^{s-1}(\Omega^{\pm})}^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-1}+\left|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\overline{\partial}{}^{s-1}b^{\pm}\cdot N\right)\right|_{0}\right).

To control the boundary norm of
\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-\frac{1}{2}}b^{\pm}\cdot N
, we again convert it to an interior integral and use the divergence constraint
0=\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot b^{\pm}=\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{b}^{\pm}+\partial^{\varphi}_{3}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}
in
\Omega^{\pm}
. Note that we may not directly use the trace lemma due to the anisotropy.

\displaystyle\left|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}b^{\pm}\cdot N\right)\right|_{0}^{2}=

\displaystyle~\mp 2\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{3}\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}b^{\pm}\cdot N\right)\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}b^{\pm}\cdot N\right)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\overset{\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}{==}

\displaystyle~\mp 2\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\partial_{3}\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}\right)\,\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-\frac{1}{2}}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mp 2\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}(\partial_{3}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N})\,\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-\frac{1}{2}}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle~\mp 2\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}b^{\pm}\cdot\partial_{3}\mathbf{N}-\left[\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1},\mathbf{N}\cdot\right]\partial_{3}b^{\pm}\right)\,\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-\frac{1}{2}}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\pm 2\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}\left(\partial_{3}\varphi(\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{b}^{\pm})\right)\,\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-\frac{1}{2}}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle~\mp 2\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}b^{\pm}\cdot\partial_{3}\mathbf{N}-\left[\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1},\mathbf{N}\cdot\right]\partial_{3}b^{\pm}\right)\,\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-\frac{1}{2}}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~P(|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}+\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}+\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-2}\partial_{3}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}\right)\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}.

∎

Lemma
4.2
shows that the
H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma)
norm of the free interface
\psi
can be converted to lower-order terms and
H_{*}^{s}(\Omega^{\pm})
norms of
b^{\pm}
. Thus, the “non-collinearity” of
b^{+}
and
b^{-}
on
\Sigma
brings a gain of
1/2
-order regularity for the interface. Given
l\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
, the definition of
\widetilde{E}_{4+l}(t)
suggests that
\varepsilon^{2l}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{2l}b^{\pm}\in H^{4-l}(\Omega^{\pm})
. Thus, letting
s=4+l
in Lemma
4.2
, we can get

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}\psi\right|_{3.5+l}^{2}\leq P(\|b^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm},|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|\varepsilon^{2l}b^{\pm}\|_{H_{*}^{4+l}(\Omega^{\pm})}\|\varepsilon^{2l}b^{\pm}\|_{H_{*}^{3+l}(\Omega^{\pm})}+|\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{3+l}^{2}+\|\varepsilon^{2l}b^{\pm}\|_{H_{*}^{4+l}(\Omega^{\pm})}^{2}\right).

Similarly, we can show the enhanced regularity for time derivatives of
\psi
after replacing
\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-\frac{1}{2}}\psi
by
\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-k-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi
. We conclude the following proposition.

Lemma 4.3
.

For
3\leq s\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
1\leq k\leq s-1,~k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, one has

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi|_{s-k-\frac{1}{2}}^{2}\leq P\left(\|b^{\pm}\|_{s-1,*,\pm},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}|\partial_{t}^{j}\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{s-j-\frac{3}{2}}^{2}+|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi|_{s-k-1}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-k}\partial_{t}^{k}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}\|\partial_{3}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-k-2}\partial_{t}^{k}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}+\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-k}\partial_{t}^{k}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{2}\bigg),

(4.10)

where the number of time derivatives in
\|b^{\pm}\|_{s,*,\pm}
appearing on the right side does not exceed
k
. The term
\partial_{3}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-k-2}\partial_{t}^{k}b^{\pm}
does not appear when
s-k=1
.

Remark 4.1
.

In the control of
\widetilde{E}_{4+l}(t)
for
0\leq l\leq 4
, we have
\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}b\in L^{2}(\Omega^{\pm})
for
0\leq k\leq l,~\langle\alpha\rangle=2l,~\alpha_{3}=0
. It should also be noted that there is no loss of Mach number in the estimates of
\partial_{t}^{k}\psi
because the number of time derivatives appearing on the right side of (
4.10
) does not exceed that on the left side. Thus, the above estimates directly help us to control the quantities ST, RT and
Z^{\pm}+ZB^{\pm}
by
P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau
uniformly in
\sigma
under the stability condition (
4.7
).

Apart from the term VS, we still need to control the commutators
\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}[\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot]f\right\|_{3-k-l}^{2}
and
\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}[\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha},\nabla^{\varphi}\times]f\right\|_{3-k-l}^{2}
for
f=v,b
and
0\leq l\leq 3,~1\leq k+l\leq 3,~\langle\alpha\rangle=2l,~\alpha_{3}=0
, in which the highest order terms have the form
\varepsilon^{2l}(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{-1}(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\varphi)(\partial_{3}f)
whose estimate requires the bound for
|\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi|_{3-k-l}^{2}
. We can also assume
\alpha_{4}=0
because
\varphi
has
C^{\infty}
-regularity in
x_{3}
-direction. Such terms appear without time integral, so we control them by
P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau
.

Letting
s=3.5+l
in Lemma
4.2
for
0\leq l\leq 4
, using interpolation and Young’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{3+l}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~P(\|b^{\pm}\|_{2+l,*,\pm},\|b^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm},|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|\partial_{3}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{1.5+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{3.5+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{2.5+l}^{2}+\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{3.5+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~P(\|b^{\pm}\|_{2+l,*,\pm},\|b^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm},|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})

\displaystyle\left(\|\partial_{3}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{1+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{3}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{2+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{3+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{4+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{2.5+l}^{2}+\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{3+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{4+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta\left(\|\partial_{3}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{2+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{4+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+P(\|b^{\pm}\|_{2+l,*,\pm},\|b^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm},|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}},\delta^{-1})\left(\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{3+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{3+l}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}+|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{2.5+l}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta\|b^{\pm}\|_{4+l,*,\pm}^{2}+P(\|b_{0}^{\pm}\|_{3+l,*,\pm},|\overline{\partial}{}\psi_{0}|_{2.5+l})+\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\|\partial_{t}b^{\pm}(\tau)\|_{3+l,*,\pm},|\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}\psi(\tau)|_{2.5+l}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Similarly, by replacing
\overline{\partial}{}\psi
with
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi
, we can get the following inequality, where
0\leq l\leq 3,~1\leq k+l\leq 3,~\langle\alpha\rangle=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}=2l~(\alpha_{3}=\alpha_{4}=0)
:

\displaystyle\left|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}\psi\right|_{3-k-l}^{2}=\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{1+2l-\alpha_{0}}\partial_{t}^{k+\alpha_{0}}\psi\right|_{3-k-l}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta\|b^{\pm}\|_{4+l,*,\pm}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{k}P\left(\|b^{\pm}\|_{3+l,*,\pm},|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{j+\alpha_{0}}\psi|_{2.5+l-j-\alpha_{0}}\right)\big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\|\partial_{t}b^{\pm}(\tau)\|_{3+l,*,\pm},|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{j+\alpha_{0}+1}\psi(\tau)|_{2.5+l-j-\alpha_{0}}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Since there is no loss of weights of Mach number when applying Lemma
4.2
-Lemma
4.3
to the estimates of compressible current-vortex sheet system (
1.15
), we can conclude the enhanced regularity, which is uniform in
(\varepsilon,\sigma)
, of the free interface by the following proposition.

Proposition 4.4
.

For
l\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
and
k\leq 4+l,~k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

1.

When
0\leq k\leq 3+l
:

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}\psi(t)\right|_{3.5+l-k}^{2}\leq P\left(\sum_{j=0}^{(l-1)_{+}}\widetilde{E}_{4+j}(0)\right)+P\left(\sum_{j=0}^{(l-1)_{+}}\widetilde{E}_{4+j}(t)\right)\left(\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l}\widetilde{E}_{4+j}(\tau)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\left\|b^{\pm}(t)\right\|_{4+l,*,\pm}^{2}\right).

(4.11)

2.

When
k=4+l
:

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\partial_{t}^{4+l}\psi(t)\right|_{0.5}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\left\|\varepsilon^{2l}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\partial_{t}^{3+l}v^{\pm}\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\varepsilon^{2l+2}\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle\partial_{t}^{3+l}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}p^{\pm}\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}\right)+|\bar{v}|_{L^{\infty}}\left|\varepsilon^{2l}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3+l}\psi(t)\right|_{0.5}

\displaystyle+P\left(\sum_{j=0}^{(l-1)_{+}}\widetilde{E}_{4+j}(0)\right)+\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l}\widetilde{E}_{4+j}(\tau)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(4.12)

Proof.

When
k\leq 3+l
, the inequality (
4.11
) is a direct consequence of Lemma
4.2
and Lemma
4.3
. Indeed, we just need to control the
\psi
term on the right side of (
4.11
)-(
4.12
) to be
P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau
. This can be done by applying again
4.2
and Lemma
4.3
to the
\psi
term appearing on the right side of (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) by replacing
s=4+l-k
with
s=3.5+l-k
. When
k=4+l
, we just differentiate the kinematic boundary condition
\partial_{t}\psi=v^{\pm}\cdot N
to get

\partial_{t}^{4+l}\psi=\partial_{t}^{3+l}v^{\pm}\cdot N+\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}^{3+l}\psi+[\partial_{t}^{3+l},v^{\pm}\cdot,N],

where the second term contributes to the second term on the right side of (
4.12
) and the last term contributes to the second line of (
4.12
). For
\partial_{t}^{3+l}v^{\pm}\cdot N
, we again use Gauss-Green formula to covert its boundary norm to an interior integral and use
\partial^{\varphi}_{3}v^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{N}=\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm}-\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}^{\pm}=-\varepsilon^{2}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}p^{\pm}-\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{v}^{\pm}
to replace the normal derivative by tangential derivative. The proof is parallel to the control of
|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}b^{\pm}\cdot N\right)|_{0}
as in the proof of Lemma
4.2
and we no longer repeat the details.
∎

4.2
Elimination of VS term: Friedrichs secondary symmetrization

The quantity ST produces the
\sqrt{\sigma}
-weighted boundary energy in
\widetilde{E}(t)
as in
[
63
, Prop. 3.3]
. With the new energy functional (
4.8
), remainder terms in ST, quantities RT and
ZB^{\pm}+Z^{\pm}
mentioned at the beginning of Section
4
are all controlled by
P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau
thanks to Proposition
4.4
. The terms that appear without time integral on the right side of (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) can also be controlled by
P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau
via div-curl analysis or tangential estimates. In other words, we have reached the following energy inequality for
\widetilde{E}(t)

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}(t)\lesssim\delta\widetilde{E}(t)+P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\text{ VS}\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(4.13)

so it remains to control or eliminate the term VS arising from the estimates of
\widetilde{E}(t)
, such that we can close the energy estimates for
\widetilde{E}(t)
and also get rid of the dependence on
1/\sigma
.

4.2.1
Motivation for Friedrichs secondary symmetrization

The regularity for the free interface needed in the control of
VS
is higher than the one we obtain in Proposition
4.4
. So, we alternatively try to completely eliminate the term VS by utilizing the jump of tangential magnetic field. Recall that the term VS is generated due to the discontinuity in tangential velocity

\text{ VS}=\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}q^{-}\,(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},\quad\quad\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}=\partial_{t}^{\gamma_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}^{\gamma_{2}}~(\gamma_{3}=\gamma_{4}=0),

in which we may try to insert a term
\left\llbracket\bar{\mu}\bar{b}\right\rrbracket
into
\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket
such that
\left\llbracket\bar{v}-\bar{\mu}\bar{b}\right\rrbracket=\mathbf{0}
on
\Sigma
for some function
\bar{\mu}^{\pm}
. Such functions
\bar{\mu}^{\pm}
do exist and are unique thanks to the non-collinearity
\bar{b}^{+}\nparallel\bar{b}^{-}
on
\Sigma
:

\begin{cases}\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket=\bar{\mu}^{+}b_{1}^{+}-\bar{\mu}^{-}b_{1}^{-}\\
\left\llbracket v_{2}\right\rrbracket=\bar{\mu}^{+}b_{2}^{+}-\bar{\mu}^{-}b_{2}^{-}\end{cases}\xRightarrow{~b^{+}\nparallel b^{-}}~\bar{\mu}^{\pm}=\dfrac{b_{1}^{\mp}\left\llbracket v_{2}\right\rrbracket-b_{2}^{\mp}\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket}{b_{1}^{+}b_{2}^{-}-b_{1}^{-}b_{2}^{+}}=\dfrac{(\bar{b}^{\mp}\times\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket)_{3}}{(\bar{b}^{+}\times\bar{b}^{-})_{3}}.

(4.14)

Next, a natural question is how to produce such
\left\llbracket\bar{\mu}\bar{b}\right\rrbracket
-terms in the tangential estimates. Recall that the discontinuity term
(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla})\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi
is produced by taking substraction bewteen the equations of
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}v^{\pm}\cdot N
which originates from
\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}v^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}
(see
[
63
, (3.33)]
for example). This suggests us to replace the variable
v^{\pm}
by
v^{\pm}-\bar{\mu}^{\pm}b^{\pm}
in the momentum equation in order to create the elimination
\left\llbracket\bar{v}-\bar{\mu}\bar{b}\right\rrbracket=\mathbf{0}
. However, such replacement in the momentum equation will make the compressible ideal MHD system (
1.15
) no longer symmetric, which will further lead to the failure of
L^{2}
energy conservation. Hence, we must re-symmetrize the hyperbolic system after replacing
v
by
v-\mu b
.

The technique we use is the so-called Friedrichs secondary symmetrization
[
20
]
. For compressible ideal MHD system, the symmetrizer was explicitly calculated in Trakhinin
[
52
]
. Let
\mu(t,x)=\bar{\mu}(t,x^{\prime})\eta(x_{3})
where
\eta(x_{3})\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is a smooth, non-negative, even function satisfying
\eta(0)=1
and
\eta(x_{3})=0
when
|x_{3}|>\delta_{1}
for some sufficiently small constant
\delta_{1}>0
. Inserting this
\eta
is to localise the function
\mu^{\pm}
near the interface
\Sigma
. The new system takes the form

\begin{cases}\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b+\nabla^{\varphi}(p+\frac{1}{2}|b|^{2})-\mu\rho\left(D_{t}^{\varphi}b-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})v+b(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)\right)=0,\\
\mathcal{F}_{p}D_{t}^{\varphi}p+\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v+\mu\mathcal{F}_{p}\left(\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v\cdot b+b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}p\right)=0\\
D_{t}^{\varphi}b-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})v+b(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)-\mu\left(\rho D_{t}^{\varphi}v-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b+\nabla^{\varphi}(p+\frac{1}{2}|b|^{2})\right)=0,\end{cases}

(4.15)

where these equations are obtained by doing the following linear transform on (
1.15
)

\displaystyle\text{new momentum equation}=

\displaystyle~\text{momentum equation}-\mu\rho(\text{evolution equation of }b),

\displaystyle\text{new continuity equation}=

\displaystyle~\text{continuity equation}+\mu\mathcal{F}_{p}(\text{momentum equation})\cdot b,

\displaystyle\text{new evolution equation of }b=

\displaystyle~\text{momentum equation}-\mu(\text{momentum equation}).

Note that in the second equation we use the fact that
\left(\nabla^{\varphi}(1/2|b|^{2})-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b\right)\cdot b=(b\times(\nabla^{\varphi}\times b))\cdot b=0
.

4.2.2
Reformulations in Alinhac good unknowns

Given a tangential derivative
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}:=(\omega(x_{3})\partial_{3})^{\gamma_{4}}\partial_{t}^{\gamma_{0}}\partial_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}\partial_{2}^{\gamma_{2}}
with
\langle\gamma\rangle=\gamma_{0}+\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{4}
., we need to re-consider the tangential estimates in order to avoid the appearance of the term VS when
\gamma_{4}=0
(When
\gamma_{4}\neq 0
, no boundary term appears thanks to
\omega(x_{3})=0
, so we can just mimic the proof in
[
63
, Section 3.3-3.4]
). We define the Alinhac good unknown of a given function
f
with respect to
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
by
\mathbf{F}^{\gamma}:=\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}f-\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\varphi\partial_{3}^{\varphi}f
. The good unknown
\mathbf{F}
satisfies

\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\nabla_{i}^{\varphi}f=\nabla^{\varphi}_{i}\mathbf{F}^{\gamma}+\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\gamma}(f),~~\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}D_{t}^{\varphi}f=D_{t}^{\varphi}\mathbf{F}^{\gamma}+\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(f),

(4.16)

where the commutators
\mathfrak{C}^{\gamma}_{i}(f)
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(f)
are defined by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\gamma}(f)=

\displaystyle~(\partial^{\varphi}_{3}\partial^{\varphi}_{i}f)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\varphi+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi},\partial_{3}f\right]+\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathbf{N}_{i},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\right]+\mathbf{N}_{i}\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\right]\mathcal{T}^{\gamma^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\varphi

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{\partial_{3}\varphi}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]f-\frac{\mathbf{N}_{i}}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\partial_{3}f[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]\varphi,\quad i=1,2,3,

(4.17)

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(f)=

\displaystyle~(D_{t}^{\varphi}\partial^{\varphi}_{3}f)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\varphi+[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\bar{v}]\cdot\overline{\partial}{}f+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi),\partial_{3}f\right]+\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi,\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\right]\partial_{3}f

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},v]\cdot\mathbf{N}\partial_{3}f-(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\partial_{3}f\left[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}},\frac{1}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}\right]\mathcal{T}^{\gamma^{\prime}}\partial_{3}\varphi

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]f+(v\cdot\mathbf{N}-\partial_{t}\varphi)\frac{\partial_{3}f}{(\partial_{3}\varphi)^{2}}[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\partial_{3}]\varphi

(4.18)

with
\langle\gamma^{\prime}\rangle=1
. Here
\mathbf{N}:=(-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\varphi,-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\varphi,1)^{\top}
is the extension of normal vector
N
in
\Omega^{\pm}
. The third term on the right side of (
4.17
) is zero when
i=3
because
\mathbf{N}_{3}=1
is a constant. One can follow
[
63
, Section 3.3-3.5]
to verify that the
L^{2}(\Omega^{\pm})
norms of commutators
\mathfrak{C}_{i}^{\gamma}(f)
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(f)
can be controlled by
P(\widetilde{E}(t))
for
f=v_{i}^{\pm}
or
q^{\pm}
by directly counting the number of derivatives.

Under the above setting and dropping the superscript
\gamma
for convenience, the
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
-differentiated current-vortex sheet system, after doing Friedrichs secondary symmetrization, is reformulated in the corresponding Alinhac good unknowns
(\mathbf{V}^{\pm},\mathbf{B}^{\pm},\mathbf{P}^{\pm},\mathbf{Q}^{\pm},\mathbf{S}^{\pm})
as follows

\displaystyle\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}-(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}-(b\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{V}^{\pm}+b^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm}))=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{R}^{\pm,\mu}_{v}-\mathfrak{C}(q^{\pm})+\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}b^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm}),

(4.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm}+\mu^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm}+b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}\mathbf{P}^{\pm})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\pm,\mu}-\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})-\mu^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathfrak{C}(q^{\pm}),

(4.20)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}-(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{V}^{\pm}+b^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})-\mu(\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}-(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm})=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\pm,\mu}-b^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})+\mu^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}(q^{\pm})

(4.21)

\displaystyle D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{S}^{\pm}=

\displaystyle~\mathfrak{D}(S^{\pm}),

(4.22)

where
\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\mu},\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\mu},\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\mu}
terms consist of the following commutators

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\pm,\mu}:=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\pm}+[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}]\left(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}b^{\pm}-(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})v^{\pm}+b^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\right)

(4.23)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\pm,\mu}:=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\pm}+\mu^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\pm}-[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mu^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}]\left(\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}v^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm}-(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})p^{\pm}\right)

(4.24)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\pm,\mu}:=

\displaystyle~\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\pm}+[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mu^{\pm}]\left(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}v^{\pm}-(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b^{\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi}q^{\pm}\right),

(4.25)

with
\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\pm},\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\pm},\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\pm}
defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},b^{\pm}]\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}b^{\pm}-[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\rho^{\pm}]D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}v^{\pm}-\rho^{\pm}\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(v^{\pm}),

(4.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\pm}:=

\displaystyle-[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}]D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}p^{\pm}-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(p^{\pm}),

(4.27)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{b}^{\pm}:=

\displaystyle~[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},b^{\pm}]\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}v^{\pm}-\mathfrak{D}^{\gamma}(b^{\pm}).

(4.28)

The boundary conditions on the interface
\Sigma
are

\displaystyle\left\llbracket\mathbf{Q}\right\rrbracket=\sigma\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\mathcal{H}(\psi)-\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi~~

\displaystyle\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,

(4.29)

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot N=\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi+\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi-\mathcal{W}_{v}^{\pm}~

\displaystyle\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,

(4.30)

\displaystyle b^{\pm}\cdot N=0\Rightarrow\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot N=\bar{b}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi-\mathcal{W}_{b}^{\pm}~

\displaystyle\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,

(4.31)

and the boundary term
\mathcal{W}^{\gamma,\pm}
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{f}^{\pm}:=(\partial_{3}f^{\pm}\cdot N)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi+[\mathcal{T}^{\gamma},N_{i},f_{i}^{\pm}],~~f=v,b.

(4.32)

Note that the good unknowns
\mathbf{V}^{\pm},\mathbf{B}^{\pm},\mathbf{Q}^{\pm},\mathbf{P}^{\pm},\mathbf{S}^{\pm}
also satisfy the above system with mathematically setting
\mu^{\pm}=0
because the secondary-symmetrized system above is obtained by doing a linear transform on the original system (
1.15
). Also note that we only need to re-consider the
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
-estimates for
\gamma_{4}=0
, we can rewrite
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
to be
\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}
for
0\leq k\leq 4+l
and
0\leq l\leq 4
. In this case, the second line in (
4.17
) and the third line in (
4.18
) are both zero because
\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4+l-k}
commutes with
\partial_{3}
.

4.2.3
Tangential estimates (I): Analysis in the interior

Recall that the term VS originates from the tangential estimates. After doing Friedrichs secondary symmtrisation, we shall consider the tangential estimates for the following functional to establish the
\varepsilon^{2l}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
-estimates
(0\leq l\leq 4,\langle\gamma\rangle=4+l)
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}^{\pm,\mu}(t):=\frac{\varepsilon^{4l}}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}|\mathbf{V}^{\pm}|^{2}+|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathbf{P}^{\pm})^{2}-2\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+2\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}(b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(4.33)

instead of the following one used in
[
63
, Section 3.3-3.5]

\mathbf{G}^{\pm}(t):=\frac{\varepsilon^{4l}}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}|\mathbf{V}^{\pm}|^{2}+|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathbf{P}^{\pm})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}.

Using Reynolds’ transport theorem (Theorem
A.3
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbf{G}^{\pm,\mu}(t)=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot(\mathbf{V}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+\varepsilon^{4l}\mu^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}b^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot(\mathbf{B}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

(4.34)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}(\mathbf{P}^{\pm}+\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\varepsilon^{4l}R_{1}^{\pm,\mu}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4l}(G_{1}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{2}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{3}^{\pm,\mu}+R_{1}^{\pm,\mu})

where

\displaystyle R_{1}^{\pm,\mu}:=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm})\left(|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}+(\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm})^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mu^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}(\rho^{\pm}b^{\pm})\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mu\left(\rho^{\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+\rho^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}(b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}.

(4.35)

Invoking the evolution equations of good unknowns with all
\mu^{\pm}
-terms dropped, we get

\displaystyle G_{1}^{\pm,\mu}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot\left(\mathbf{V}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\nabla^{\varphi}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}\cdot\left(\mathbf{V}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mu^{\pm}(\rho^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm})\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathfrak{C}(q^{\pm})\cdot\left(\mathbf{V}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\pm}\cdot\left(\mathbf{V}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~G_{11}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{12}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{13}^{\pm,\mu}+R_{2}^{\pm,\mu}+R_{3}^{\pm,\mu}.

(4.36)

In
G_{11}^{\pm,\mu}
and
G_{12}^{\pm,\mu}
, we integrate by parts to get

\displaystyle G_{11}^{\pm,\mu}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot(\mu^{\pm}b^{\pm})|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~G_{111}^{\pm,\mu}+R_{4}^{\pm,\mu}

(4.37)

and use
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}=-\mathfrak{C}_{i}(b_{i}^{\pm})
to get

\displaystyle G_{12}^{\pm,\mu}=

\displaystyle\pm\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}(\mathbf{V}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\mathbf{B}^{\pm})\cdot N\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mu^{\pm}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}\,\mathfrak{C}_{i}(b_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~G_{0}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{121}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{122}^{\pm,\mu}.

(4.38)

In
G_{13}^{\pm,\mu}
, we notice that

(-(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+\nabla^{\varphi}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}_{j}b_{j}^{\pm})\cdot b^{\pm}=-b_{j}^{\pm}(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\pm})b_{i}^{\pm}+(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}_{j})b_{j}^{\pm}b_{i}^{\pm}=0,

so it becomes the following controllable quantities by using symmetry

\displaystyle G_{13}^{\pm,\mu}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}((b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{P}^{\pm})\,\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mu^{\pm}(\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\pm}-\mathfrak{C}(q^{\pm}))\cdot b^{\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}b^{\pm})(\mathbf{P}^{\pm})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mu^{\pm}(\mathcal{R}_{v}^{\pm}-\mathfrak{C}(q^{\pm}))\cdot b^{\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=:R_{5}^{\pm,\mu}+R_{6}^{\pm,\mu}.

(4.39)

Note that the terms
G_{111}^{\pm,\mu}
and
G_{122}^{\pm,\mu}
already appear in the previous analysis for (
1.15
) in
[
63
, Section 3.3.1]
, so we no longer need to put extra effort on it.

Next we analyze
G_{2}^{\pm,\mu}\sim G_{3}^{\pm,\mu}
. Invoking equations of
\mathbf{P}^{\pm}
and
\mathbf{B}^{\pm}
with all
\mu^{\pm}
-terms dropped, we get

\displaystyle G_{2}^{\pm,\mu}=

\displaystyle~\underbrace{\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}((b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{=-G_{111}^{\pm,\mu}}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}b^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot b^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle-G_{111}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{21}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{22}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{23}^{\pm,\mu}+R_{7}^{\pm,\mu}+R_{8}^{\pm,\mu},

(4.40)

and

\displaystyle G_{3}^{\pm,\mu}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}\underbrace{-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}\mu^{\pm}b^{\pm}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}}_{=-G_{23}^{\pm,\mu}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\pm}+\mu^{\pm}(\mathcal{R}_{p}^{\pm}-\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm}))(\rho^{\pm}b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~G_{31}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{32}^{\pm,\mu}-G_{23}^{\pm,\mu}+R_{9}^{\pm,\mu}.

(4.41)

Now we can see a lot of cancellation structures among these interior integrals. First, using

\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}=\mathbf{P}^{\pm}+b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm},\quad\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}=\sum\limits_{1\leq\langle\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\leq\langle\gamma\rangle-1}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}b_{j}^{\pm}\cdot\mathcal{T}^{\gamma-\gamma^{\prime}}b^{\pm}_{j},

we have

\displaystyle G_{121}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{21}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{31}^{\pm,\mu}=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}=:R_{10}^{\pm,\mu},

(4.42)

\displaystyle G_{122}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{22}^{\pm,\mu}+G_{32}^{\pm,\mu}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}(\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})-\mu\mathfrak{C}_{i}(b_{i}^{\pm}))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathcal{R}_{q}^{\pm}(\mathfrak{C}_{i}(v_{i}^{\pm})-\mu\mathfrak{C}_{i}(b_{i}^{\pm}))\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}

\displaystyle=:\varepsilon^{-4l}Z^{\pm,\mu}+R_{11}^{\pm,\mu}.

(4.43)

Here we add
\varepsilon^{-4l}
to the first term just for the consistency of notations. The terms
R_{j}^{\pm,\mu}~(1\leq j\leq 11)
can be directly controlled using the same method in
[
63
, Section 3.3-3.4]
, so we omit the details.

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=1}^{11}R_{j}^{\pm,\mu}\leq\delta P(\widetilde{E}(t))+P(\widetilde{E}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad\forall\delta\in(0,1).

(4.44)

Thus, it again remains to analyze the boundary integral
G_{0}^{\pm,\mu}
and the commutator term
Z^{\pm,\mu}
.

4.2.4
Tangential estimates (II): Elimination of the term VS

Invoking the boundary conditions, the boundary integral
G_{0}^{\pm,\mu}
can be decomposed as follows

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4l}(G_{0}^{+,\mu}+G_{0}^{-,\mu})=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{+}(\mathbf{V}^{+}-\mu^{+}\mathbf{B}^{+})\cdot N\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}-\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{-}(\mathbf{V}^{-}-\mu^{-}\mathbf{B}^{-})\cdot N\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\text{ ST}^{\mu}+{\text{ ST}^{\mu}}^{\prime}+\text{ VS}^{\mu}+\text{ RT}^{\mu}+\text{ RT}^{\pm,\mu}+ZB^{\pm,\mu}

(4.45)

where

\displaystyle\text{ ST}^{\mu}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\,\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.46)

\displaystyle{\text{ ST}^{\mu}}^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket\,\left((\bar{v}^{+}-\bar{\mu}^{+}\bar{b}^{+})\cdot\overline{\nabla}\right)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.47)

\displaystyle\text{ VS}^{\mu}:=

\displaystyle~\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}q^{-}\,\left(\left\llbracket\bar{v}-\bar{\mu}\bar{b}\right\rrbracket\cdot\overline{\nabla}\right)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.48)

\displaystyle\text{ RT}^{\mu}:=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.49)

\displaystyle\text{ RT}^{\pm,\mu}:=

\displaystyle\mp\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{3}q^{\pm}\,\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\left((\bar{v}^{\pm}-\bar{\mu}^{\pm}\bar{b}^{\pm})\cdot\overline{\nabla}\right)\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(4.50)

\displaystyle ZB^{\pm,\mu}:=

\displaystyle\mp\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Sigma}\mathbf{Q}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}_{v}^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}\mathcal{W}_{b}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.51)

Among the terms (
4.46
)-(
4.51
),
\text{ ST}^{\mu},{\text{ ST}^{\mu}}^{\prime},\text{ RT}^{\mu},\text{ RT}^{\pm,\mu}
can be analyzed in the same way as in
[
63
, Section 3.3-3.4]
, so we no longer repeat the proof.

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma\varepsilon^{4l}}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\psi|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{t}\text{ ST}^{\mu}+{\text{ ST}^{\mu}}^{\prime}+\text{ RT}^{\mu}+\text{ RT}^{\pm,\mu}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\delta\widetilde{E}(t)+P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Also, the control of these terms do not depend on
1/\sigma
thanks to the enhanced regularity of
\psi
obtained in Section
4.1
.

With the unique choice of
\mu^{\pm}
in (
4.14
), the quantity
\bar{v}-\mu\bar{b}
has NO jump across the interface
\Sigma
and so
\text{ VS}^{\mu}=0
.
Finally, there is a cancellation structure in
ZB^{\pm,\mu}+Z^{\pm,\mu}
which is similar to the one observed in step 4 of Section 3.3.1 and step 4 of Section 3.4.3 in
[
63
]
. It suffices to replace
v^{\pm}
by
v^{\pm}-\mu^{\pm}b^{\pm}
and use
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot b^{\pm}=0
in
\Omega^{\pm}
in order for the same result.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}ZB^{\pm,\mu}+Z^{\pm,\mu}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\delta\widetilde{E}(t)+P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

4.2.5
The stability condition ensures the hyperbolicity

So far, we have obtained the following estimates for
\mathbf{G}^{\pm,\mu}(t)
in tangential estimates:

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\frac{\varepsilon^{4l}}{2}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}|\mathbf{V}^{\pm}|^{2}+|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathbf{P}^{\pm})^{2}-2\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+2\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}(b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\frac{\sigma\varepsilon^{4l}}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.52)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{E}(t)+P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Compared to the tangential estimates in
[
63
, Section 3.3-3.4]
, we must guarantee the
positive-definiteness
of

\displaystyle|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}|^{2}+|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathbf{P}^{\pm})^{2}-2\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+2\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}(b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})

as a quadratic form of
(\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}\mathbf{V}^{\pm},\mathbf{B}^{\pm},\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm})
in
\Omega^{\pm}
, respectively. We have

\displaystyle|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}|^{2}+|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathbf{P}^{\pm})^{2}-2\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\pm}+2\mu^{\pm}\rho^{\pm}\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}(b^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\pm})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}|^{2}+|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathbf{P}^{\pm})^{2}-2\left|\mu^{\pm}\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}\right|\left|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\right|\left|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}\right|-2\left|\mu^{\pm}\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}\right|\frac{c_{A}^{\pm}}{c_{s}^{\pm}}\left|\sqrt{\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}}\mathbf{P}^{\pm}\right|\left|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\right|

Here we use the fact that
\mathcal{F}_{p}=1/(\rho c_{s}^{2})
and
c_{A}:=|b|/\sqrt{\rho}
. Therefore, we woule require that the following matrix only has strictly positive eigenvalues

\begin{pmatrix}1&-|\mu^{\pm}|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}&-|\mu^{\pm}|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}\dfrac{c_{A}^{\pm}}{c_{s}^{\pm}}\\
-|\mu^{\pm}|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}&1&0\\
-|\mu^{\pm}|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}\dfrac{c_{A}^{\pm}}{c_{s}^{\pm}}&0&1\end{pmatrix},

which further requires the following inequalities

(\mu^{\pm})^{2}\rho^{\pm}\left(1+\left(\frac{c_{A}^{\pm}}{c_{s}^{\pm}}\right)^{2}\right)<1\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm}\Rightarrow|\bar{b}^{+}\times\bar{b}^{-}|>|\bar{b}^{\mp}\times\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}\left(1+\left(\frac{c_{A}^{\pm}}{c_{s}^{\pm}}\right)^{2}\right)}\text{ on }\Sigma.

Thus, we find that the stability condition (
1.27
), namely

\exists\delta_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{8}),\text{ such that }(1-\delta_{0})|\bar{b}^{+}\times\bar{b}^{-}|\geq|\bar{b}^{\mp}\times\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket|\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}\left(1+\left(\frac{c_{A}^{\pm}}{c_{s}^{\pm}}\right)^{2}\right)}>0\text{ holds on }\Sigma,

exactly ensures the positive-definiteness of
\mathbf{G}^{\pm,\mu}(t)
. Plugging this into the energy inequality (
4.53
), we find that there exists some constant
\delta_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{8})
, such that the following estimate holds for the Alinhac good unknowns with respect to the tangential derivative
\varepsilon^{4l}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}~(0\leq l\leq 4,\langle\gamma\rangle=4+l)
.

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}\frac{\delta_{0}}{2}\varepsilon^{4l}\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\rho^{\pm}|\mathbf{V}^{\pm}|^{2}+|\mathbf{B}^{\pm}|^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(\mathbf{P}^{\pm})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}+\frac{\sigma\varepsilon^{4l}}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\frac{|\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}{{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(4.53)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta\widetilde{E}(t)+P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}/100).

Finally, we just use the definition of Alinhac good unknowns to recover the
\varepsilon^{2l}
-weighted
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}
-tangential estimates as recorded in Proposition
4.1
by following the argument in
[
63
, Section 3.5]
.

4.3
Incompressible and zero-surface-tension-limits under the stability condition

Combining the tangential estimates (
4.53
), the enhanced regularity for
\psi
obtained in Section
4.1
and the div-curl analysis recorded in Section
3
, we conclude the uniform-in-
(\varepsilon,\sigma)
estimates of
\widetilde{E}(t)
by

\displaystyle\forall\delta\in(0,\frac{\delta_{0}}{100}),\quad\widetilde{E}(t)\lesssim\delta\widetilde{E}(t)+P(\widetilde{E}(0))+P(\widetilde{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(4.54)

Using Gronwall-type argument, we know there exists some
T>0
independent of
({\varepsilon,\sigma})
such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\widetilde{E}(t)\leq P(\widetilde{E}(0)).

(4.55)

With the uniform-in-(
{\varepsilon,\sigma}
) estimates, we can pass the limit
{\varepsilon,\sigma}\to 0_{+}
to the incompressible current-vortex sheets problem without surface tension under the non-collinearity condition. Given
{\varepsilon,\sigma}>0
, let
(\psi^{\varepsilon,\sigma},v^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},b^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},\rho^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},S^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}})
be the solution to (
1.15
) with initial data
(\psi_{0}^{\varepsilon,\sigma},v_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},b_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},\rho_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},S_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}})
and let
(\xi^{0},w^{\pm,0},h^{\pm,0},\mathfrak{S}^{\pm,0})
be the solution to (
1.32
) with
\sigma=0
with initial data
(\xi_{0}^{0},w_{0}^{\pm,0},h_{0}^{\pm,0},\mathfrak{S}_{0}^{\pm,0})
. We assume

a.

(\psi_{0}^{\varepsilon,\sigma},v_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},b_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},S_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}})\in H^{9.5}(\Sigma)\times H_{*}^{8}(\Omega^{\pm})\times H_{*}^{8}(\Omega^{\pm})\times H_{*}^{8}(\Omega^{\pm})
satisfies the compatibility conditions (
1.16
) up to 7-th order, the stability condition (
1.29
) and
|\psi_{0}^{\varepsilon,\sigma}|_{\infty}\leq 1
.

b.

(\psi_{0}^{\varepsilon,\sigma},v_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},b_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},S_{0}^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}})\to(\xi_{0}^{0},w_{0}^{\pm,0},h_{0}^{\pm,0},\mathfrak{S}_{0}^{\pm,0})
in
H^{4.5}(\Sigma)\times H^{4}(\Omega^{\pm})\times H^{4}(\Omega^{\pm})\times H^{4}(\Omega^{\pm})
as
\varepsilon,\sigma\to 0
.

c.

The incompressible initial data satisfies the constraints
\nabla^{\xi_{0}}\cdot h_{0}^{\pm}=0
in
\Omega^{\pm}
,
h^{\pm}\cdot N^{0}|_{\{t=0\}\times\Sigma}=0
, the stability condition

\displaystyle 2\delta_{0}\leq\sqrt{\mathfrak{R}_{0}^{\pm}}\left|\bar{h}_{0}^{\mp}\times[\bar{w}_{0}]\right|\leq(1-2\delta_{0})|\bar{h}_{0}^{+}\times\bar{h}_{0}^{-}|~~\text{ on }\Sigma,

(4.56)

where
\delta_{0}>0
is the same constant as in (
1.29
).

Then, by the Aubin-Lions compactness lemma, it holds that

\displaystyle(\psi^{\varepsilon,\sigma},v^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},b^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}},S^{\pm,{\varepsilon,\sigma}})\to(\xi^{0},w^{\pm,0},h^{\pm,0},\mathfrak{S}^{\pm,0}),

(4.57)

weakly-* in
L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{4.5}(\Sigma)\times(H^{4}(\Omega^{\pm}))^{3})
and strongly in
C([0,T];H_{\text{loc}}^{4.5-\delta}(\Sigma)\times(H_{\text{loc}}^{4-\delta}(\Omega^{\pm}))^{3})
after possibly passing to a subsequence. Theorem
1.2
is proven.

4.4
Double limits in 2D: a subsonic zone

When the space dimension
d=2
, the substantial part of the proof for well-posedness, uniform estimates and limit process remains unchanged. In fact, we shall only re-consider the following aspects

•

The curl operator now becomes
\nabla^{\varphi,\perp}\cdot:=(-\partial^{\varphi}_{2},\partial^{\varphi}_{1})\cdot
, so we need to check the special structure given by Lorentz force in the vorticity analysis. This has been analyzed in
[
63
, Section 3.6.4]
.

•

The interface is now a 1D curve instead of a 2D surface, thus it is impossible to have “non-parallel” magnetic fields
b^{\pm}
on
\Sigma
. The functions
\mu^{\pm}
are no longer uniquely determined by
b_{1}^{\pm}
.

We prove the following lemma about the choice of Friedrichs symmetrizer.

Lemma 4.5
.

There exist functions
\bar{\mu}^{\pm}(t,x_{1})
satisfying

\left\llbracket v_{1}-\bar{\mu}b_{1}\right\rrbracket=0~\text{ and }~|\bar{\mu}^{\pm}|<1/a^{\pm}~~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma,~~~a^{\pm}:=\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}\left(1+(c_{A}^{\pm}/c_{s}^{\pm})^{2}\right)}

if and only if the following inequality holds

\displaystyle|\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket|<\frac{|b_{1}^{+}|}{a^{+}}+\frac{|b_{1}^{-}|}{a^{-}}~~~\text{ on }[0,T]\times\Sigma.

(4.58)

Under (
4.58
), the functions
\bar{\mu}^{\pm}
are chosen to be

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}^{\pm}=\pm\frac{\text{sgn}(b_{1}^{\pm})\,a^{\mp}\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket}{a^{-}|b_{1}^{+}|+a^{+}|b_{1}^{-}|}.

(4.59)

Proof.

First, we shall exclude the possibility for
b_{1}^{\pm}=0
at some point
(x_{1},0)\in\Sigma
because of
\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket\neq 0
everywhere on
\Sigma
.

Case 1:

One of the two magnetic fields is vanishing on
\Sigma
, e.g., we assume
|b_{1}^{+}|>0=|b_{1}^{-}|
on
\Sigma
, then
\left\llbracket v_{1}-\bar{\mu}b_{1}\right\rrbracket=0
directly gives us
\bar{\mu}^{+}=\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket/b_{1}^{+}
and
\bar{\mu}^{-}
can be any function satisfying the hyperbolicity constraint
|\bar{\mu}^{-}|<1/a^{-}
. For simplicity, we may choose
\bar{\mu}^{-}=0
. Solving the constraint
|\bar{\mu}^{+}|<1/a^{+}
gives us
|\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket|<|b_{1}^{+}|/a^{+}
as a special case of (
4.58
). Similarly, when
|b_{1}^{-}|>0=|b_{1}^{+}|
on
\Sigma
, we can choose
\bar{\mu}^{-}=-\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket/b_{1}^{-}
and
\bar{\mu}^{+}
can be any function satisfying the hyperbolicity constraint
|\bar{\mu}^{+}|<1/a^{+}
and we may choose
\bar{\mu}^{+}=0
for simplicity.

Remark 4.2
.

One can verify that if
b_{0}=0
on
\Sigma
, then
b
must be identically zero on
\Sigma
. In fact, restricting the equation of
b
onto
\Sigma
and doing
L^{2}
estimate shows that
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|b^{\pm}|_{0}^{2}\leq C|\partial v|_{L^{\infty}}|b^{\pm}|_{0}^{2}
. Using Gronwall’s inequality and
b_{0}|_{\Sigma}=0
yields the result.

Case 2:

b_{1}^{\pm}
are not identically zero on
\Sigma
.
In this case, we may assume
|b_{1}^{\pm}|>0
on
\Sigma
as well. In fact, if
b_{1}^{-}
vanishes at some point
(x_{1},0)\in\Sigma
, then we can follow the choice of
\bar{\mu}^{\pm}
as in case 1 to determine the function
\bar{\mu}^{\pm}
at this point. Note that the functions
\bar{\mu}^{+}=\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket/b_{1}^{+},~\bar{\mu}^{-}=0
still satisfies (
4.59
), so they do not break the continuity and differentiability of (
4.59
) at the points where one of
b_{1}^{\pm}
vanishes. Thus, making the assumption
|b_{1}^{\pm}|>0
on
\Sigma
is reasonable.

The “if” part is easy to prove. Indeed, when the stability condition (
4.58
) holds on
\Sigma
, we can set
\bar{\mu}^{\pm}
as in (
4.59
). The direct computation shows that such
\bar{\mu}^{\pm}
satisfy
\left\llbracket v_{1}-\bar{\mu}b_{1}\right\rrbracket=0
on
\Sigma
and
|\bar{\mu}^{\pm}|<1/a^{\pm}
. Let us prove the “only if” part. When
|b_{1}^{\pm}|>0
on
\Sigma
, we can write

\bar{\mu}^{+}=\frac{\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket+\bar{\mu}^{-}b_{1}^{-}}{b_{1}^{+}}.

Using
|\bar{\mu}^{+}|<1/a^{+}
, we can solve the inequality by

-\frac{1}{a^{+}}-\frac{\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket}{b_{1}^{+}}<\frac{b_{1}^{-}}{b_{1}^{+}}\bar{\mu}^{-}<\frac{1}{a^{+}}-\frac{\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket}{b_{1}^{+}},

where
\bar{\mu}^{-}
should also satisfy
|\bar{\mu}^{-}|<1/a^{-}
. Assume
b_{1}^{\pm}<0
for simplicity (that is, the horizontal directions of
b^{\pm}
on
\Sigma
are the same). Combining these two requirements, we find that the following inequality is necessary

\displaystyle|\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket|<-\frac{b_{1}^{+}}{a^{+}}-\frac{b_{1}^{-}}{a^{-}}=\frac{|b_{1}^{+}|}{a^{+}}+\frac{|b_{1}^{-}|}{a^{-}}.

(4.60)

Similar calculation for the case
b_{1}^{\pm}>0
and the case
b_{1}^{+}b_{1}^{-}<0
also leads to the same inequality as above.
∎

5
Incompressible limit for vortex-sheet problems: A paradifferential approach

Recall that the uniform-in-
{\varepsilon,\sigma}
control of
\widetilde{E}(t)
requires
\partial_{t}^{k}v|_{t=0}=O(1)
for
0\leq k\leq 4
, while the usual definition of “well-prepared initial data” only requires
\partial_{t}v|_{t=0}=O(1)
and
\nabla^{\varphi_{0}}\cdot v_{0}=O(\varepsilon)
. In this final section, we aim to drop the uniform boundedness (in
\varepsilon
) assumption for
\geq 2
time derivatives. Compared with the energy
E(t)
that we use to prove the local existence in
[
63
]
, a new difficulty arises in the control of the “weaker” energy
\mathfrak{E}(t)
: There exhibits a loss of weight of Mach number in
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}
-tangential estimates when analyzing
\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)
. In particular, we have to control the following quantity in the cancellation structure in
Z^{\pm}+ZB^{\pm}
,

\int_{\Omega}(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}v_{i})(\overline{\partial}{}\mathbf{N}_{i})(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}q)\,\mathrm{d}x,\text{ which arises from }\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}(\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}v_{i}^{\pm})[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\mathbf{N}_{i},\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}q]\,\mathrm{d}x.

In this term,
\partial_{t}q
has to be uniformly bounded with respect to Mach number. However, now we only have
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v=O(\varepsilon)
and
\partial_{t}q=O(1/\varepsilon)
, which leads to a loss of
\varepsilon
-weight. Besides, similar difficulty also appears in the control of
-\int_{\Omega^{\pm}}\mathbf{V}^{\pm}\cdot\mathfrak{C}(q^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{V}_{t}
. Indeed, such loss of
\varepsilon
-weight necessarily happens in
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}
-tangential estimates because of the following two reasons

1.

\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}q
needs one more
\varepsilon
-weight than
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}v
;

2.

The (extension of) normal vector
\mathbf{N}
, which arises from the commutator
[\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t},\mathbf{N}_{i}/\partial_{3}\varphi,\partial_{3}f]
in
\mathfrak{C}_{i}(f)
, may NOT absorb a time derivative.

As we can see,
this type of difficulty never appears in the fixed-domain setting because the commutator terms in
\mathfrak{C}(f)
are contributed by the free-interface motion.
Unless the fluid is one-phase and the surface tension is neglected
[
62
]
, such essential difficulty cannot be avoided as long as we apply the div-curl inequality (
B.1
) to
v_{t},b_{t}
. To get rid of the loss of Mach number, we have to find a new way to control
v_{t}
,
b_{t}
and also avoid the appearance of
|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi|_{3}
without
\varepsilon
-weight.

5.1
The weaker energy for the improved incompressible limit

As in (
1.38
)-(
1.39
), we now assume the fluid to be isentropic and consider the new energy functional

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}(t):=\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)+E_{5}(t)+E_{6}(t)+E_{7}(t)+E_{8}(t)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(t):=\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t)+\widetilde{E}_{5}(t)+\widetilde{E}_{6}(t)+\widetilde{E}_{7}(t)+\widetilde{E}_{8}(t)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)=\sum_{\pm}

\displaystyle\left\|(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},p^{\pm})\right\|_{4,\pm}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi\right|_{5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{\frac{1}{2}}p^{\pm})\right\|_{3,\pm}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=2}^{4}\left\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{k}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm},(\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm})^{\frac{(k-3)_{+}}{2}}p^{\pm})\right\|_{4-k,\pm}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\right|_{5-k}^{2}

(5.1)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t)=\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)+\left|\psi\right|_{4.5}^{2}+\left|\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{3.5}^{2}+\left|\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right|_{2.5}^{2}+\left|\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{3}\psi\right|_{1.5}^{2}+\left|\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{4}\psi\right|_{0.5}^{2}.

(5.2)

We aim to prove uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimates for
\mathfrak{E}(t)
for each
\sigma>0
and prove uniform-in-
({\varepsilon,\sigma})
estimates for
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(t)
under the stability condition (
1.27
) (replaced with (
1.35
) in the 2D case). Let us analyze the estimates for different
k
in
\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t)
.

The case
k=0

When
k=0
, the reduction of
v,b
is the same as in Section
3
. That is, we use the div-curl analysis to convert normal derivatives to tangential derivatives

\displaystyle\|(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\|_{4,\pm}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\psi|_{4},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|v^{\pm},b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm},\nabla^{\varphi}\times(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{3,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{0,\pm}^{2}\right),

(5.3)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v^{\pm}\right\|_{3,\pm}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle~\left\|\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}p^{\pm}\right\|_{3,\pm}^{2},\quad\left\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times(v^{\pm},b^{\pm})\right\|_{3,\pm}^{2}\lesssim~\delta\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(\tau))+E_{5}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla q\right\|_{3,\pm}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle~\left\|\rho D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}v^{\pm}\right\|_{3,\pm}^{2}+\left\|(b^{\pm}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi})b^{\pm}\right\|_{3,\pm}^{2}.

(5.5)

The
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
-control is proved in almost the same way as in
[
63
, Section 3.3]
which gives the control of
\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\overline{\nabla}\psi\right|_{4}^{2}
. The only difference is the treatment of
RT
because we need to avoid using
\sqrt{\sigma}
-weight enenrgy when taking the limit
\sigma\to 0
. Using Kato-Ponce type product estimate (
B.6
) in Lemma
B.6
, we have

\displaystyle\text{ RT}=\int_{\Sigma}\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi_{t}\leq\left|\left\llbracket\partial_{3}q\right\rrbracket\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\psi\right|_{\frac{1}{2}}|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}\leq\|q^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm}|\psi|_{4.5}|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}.

(5.6)

So, we need to find
({\varepsilon,\sigma})
-independent control of
\left|\psi\right|_{4.5}^{2}
and
\left|\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{3.5}^{2}
.

The case
k=1

When
k=1
, we cannot use the above div-curl inequality because we must avoid
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}
-estimate. Instead, we use the div-curl inequality (
B.2
) to get

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t}v^{\pm},\partial_{t}b^{\pm})\|_{3,\pm}^{2}\leq C(|\psi|_{3.5},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\bigg(

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}v^{\pm},\partial_{t}b^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot(\partial_{t}v^{\pm},\partial_{t}b^{\pm})\right\|_{2,\pm}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times(\partial_{t}v^{\pm},\partial_{t}b^{\pm})\right\|_{2,\pm}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|\partial_{t}v^{\pm}\cdot N,\partial_{t}b^{\pm}\cdot N\right|_{2.5}^{2}\bigg).

(5.7)

The divergence part and the curl part are controlled in the same way as
[
63
, Section 3.6.1]
, so we do not repeat the analysis here. The boundary normal trace for
b_{t}
is easy to control. Using
b^{\pm}\cdot N=0
, we have
b_{t}\cdot N=\bar{b}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t}
and thus

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}b^{\pm}\cdot N\right|_{2.5}^{2}=\left|\bar{b}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t}\right|_{2.5}^{2}\lesssim\|b^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm}^{2}|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}^{2}.

(5.8)

For the normal trace
\left|\partial_{t}v^{\pm}\cdot N\right|_{2.5}^{2}
, we invoke the kinematic boundary condition
\partial_{t}\psi=v^{\pm}\cdot N
to get

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}v^{\pm}\cdot N\right|_{2.5}^{2}\leq\left|\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right|_{2.5}^{2}+\left|\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t}\right|_{2.5}^{2}\lesssim\left|\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right|_{2.5}^{2}+\|v^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm}^{2}|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}^{2}.

(5.9)

Since we avoid
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}
-tangential estimates, we must seek for another way to find
\varepsilon
-independent estimates for
\left|\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right|_{2.5}^{2}
and
\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{4}^{2}
. Also, under the stability condition (
1.27
), we need to find
({\varepsilon,\sigma})
-independent control of
\left|\psi\right|_{4.5}^{2},\left|\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{3.5}^{2},\left|\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right|_{2.5}^{2}
and
\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{4}^{2}
.

The case
2\leq k\leq 4

When
k=2,3,4
, the reduction stays the same as in
[
63
, Section 3.3-3.5]
(for the incompressible limit for fixed
\sigma>0
) and the analysis in Section
4.2
. The reason is that
\partial_{t}^{k}q
share the same weight of Mach number as
\partial_{t}^{k}v
which helps us avoid the loss of
\varepsilon
-weight in
\mathfrak{C}(q)
.

5.2
The evolution equation of the free interface and its paralinearization

To prove the uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimates for
\mathfrak{E}(t)
and the uniform-in-
({\varepsilon,\sigma})
estimates for
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(t)
under the stability condition (
1.27
), it remains to prove the
\varepsilon
-independent control of
|\psi|_{4.5},~|\psi_{t}|_{3.5},~|\psi_{tt}|_{2.5}
and
|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi_{t}|_{4}
by
P(\mathfrak{E}(0))+P(\mathfrak{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\mathfrak{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau
and
({\varepsilon,\sigma})
-independent control of them by
P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(0))+P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau
. Since we must avoid
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}
-tangential estimates, we shall further analyze the evolution equation of the free interface.

5.2.1
Derivation of the equation

We take
\partial_{t}
in the kinematic boundary condition to get
\partial_{t}^{2}\psi=\partial_{t}v^{\pm}\cdot N-\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi
. Plugging the momentum equation of (
1.15
) into the term
\partial_{t}v^{\pm}\cdot N
, we get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v^{\pm}\cdot N=-\frac{1}{\rho^{\pm}}N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q^{\pm}-(\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})v^{\pm}\cdot N+\frac{1}{\rho^{\pm}}(\bar{b}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})b^{\pm}\cdot N\quad\text{ on }\Sigma.

Using
\partial_{t}\psi=v^{\pm}\cdot N
and
b^{\pm}\cdot N=0
on
\Sigma
, we have

\displaystyle-(\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})v^{\pm}\cdot N=

\displaystyle-(\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}\psi+v^{\pm}\cdot(\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})N=-\bar{v}_{j}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}\psi-\bar{v}_{i}^{\pm}\bar{v}_{j}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi,

\displaystyle(\bar{b}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})b^{\pm}\cdot N=

\displaystyle~\bar{b}_{i}^{\pm}\bar{b}_{j}^{\pm}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi,

and thus

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{2}\psi=-\frac{1}{\rho^{\pm}}N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q^{\pm}+\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{\pm}}\bar{b}_{i}^{\pm}\bar{b}_{j}^{\pm}-\bar{v}_{i}^{\pm}\bar{v}_{j}^{\pm}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi-2(\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}\psi.

(5.10)

Next we want to separate the boundary value of
q^{\pm}
from its interior contribution in order to create an energy term involving the surface tension. First, taking
\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot
in the momentum equation and invoking the continuity equation in (
1.15
), we derive a wave-type equation

\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}p^{\pm}-\Delta^{\varphi}q^{\pm}=(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}v_{j}^{\pm})(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}v_{i}^{\pm})-(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}b_{j}^{\pm})(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}b_{i}^{\pm}),

which can be written as a wave equation of
q^{\pm}
thanks to
q^{\pm}=p^{\pm}+\frac{1}{2}|b^{\pm}|^{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}q^{\pm}-\Delta^{\varphi}q^{\pm}=\varepsilon^{2}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}|b^{\pm}|^{2}\right)+(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}v_{j}^{\pm})(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}v_{i}^{\pm})-(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}b_{j}^{\pm})(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}b_{i}^{\pm})\quad\text{ in }[0,T]\times\Omega^{\pm},

(5.11)

with a jump condition
\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket=\sigma\mathcal{H}(\psi)
on
\Sigma
and a Neumann-type boundary condition
\partial_{3}q^{\pm}=0
on
\Sigma^{\pm}
(got by restricting the momentum equations on
\Sigma^{\pm}
), where we omit the terms in which
D_{t}^{\varphi\pm}
falls on
\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}
.

Definition 5.1

(
Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator
)
.

For a function
f:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
, we now define the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator with respect to
(\psi,\Omega^{\pm})
by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}f:=\mp N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}(\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f),

(5.12)

where
\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f
is defined to be the harmonic extension of
f
into
\Omega^{\pm}
, namely

-\Delta^{\varphi}(\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f)=0\quad\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm},\quad\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f=f\quad\text{ on }\Sigma,\quad\partial_{3}(\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f)=0\quad\text{ on }\Sigma^{\pm}.

(5.13)

Thus, we can define a decomposition
q^{\pm}=q_{\psi}^{\pm}+q_{w}^{\pm}
satisfying

q_{\psi}^{\pm}:=\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}(q^{\pm}|_{\Sigma})\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm}

(5.14)

and

-\Delta^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}=-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}q^{\pm}+\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}(D_{t}^{\varphi\pm})^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}|b^{\pm}|^{2}\right)+(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}v_{j}^{\pm})(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}v_{i}^{\pm})-(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}b_{j}^{\pm})(\partial^{\varphi}_{j}b_{i}^{\pm})\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm}

(5.15)

with boundary conditions
q_{w}^{\pm}=0\text{ on }\Sigma
and
N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}=\partial_{3}q_{w}^{\pm}=0
on
\Sigma^{\pm}
. The second boundary conditions holds thanks to the slip condition for
v_{3}^{\pm},b_{3}^{\pm}
on
\Sigma^{\pm}
. Thus, the evolution equation of
\psi
can be written as

\displaystyle\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi=

\displaystyle\pm\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}(q^{\pm}|_{\Sigma})-N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}+\left(\bar{b}_{i}^{\pm}\bar{b}_{j}^{\pm}-\rho^{\pm}\bar{v}_{i}^{\pm}\bar{v}_{j}^{\pm}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi-2(\rho^{\pm}\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}\psi.

(5.16)

We now want to resolve
q^{\pm}|_{\Sigma}
in terms of
\rho^{\pm}
and
F_{\psi}^{\pm}
by inverting the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operators
\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}
. However, we no longer have
\int_{\Sigma}\rho\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=\int_{\Sigma}\rho\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=0
due to the compressibility of fluids. Thus, we have to eliminate the zero-frequency part in
\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi
before inverting the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operators. In other words, we cannot directly apply
(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm})^{-1}
to
(1/\rho)\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}(q^{\pm}|_{\Sigma})
.

For a function
f:\Sigma=\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
, we define the Littlewood-Paley projection

\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}f:=f-(f)_{\Sigma},~~(f)_{\Sigma}:=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}f\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

Under this setting, we have

\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi=\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)+(\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)_{\Sigma}

(5.17)

and we insert it back to the evolution equation to get

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)=

\displaystyle~\pm\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}(q^{\pm}|_{\Sigma})-(\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)_{\Sigma}-N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}+\left(\bar{b}_{i}^{\pm}\bar{b}_{j}^{\pm}-\rho^{\pm}\bar{v}_{i}^{\pm}\bar{v}_{j}^{\pm}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi-2(\rho^{\pm}\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}\psi

(5.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:\pm\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}(q^{\pm}|_{\Sigma})+F_{\psi}^{\pm}.

(5.19)

Note that the zero-frequency modes of both
\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)
and
\pm\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}(q^{\pm}|_{\Sigma})
are vanishing on the interface
\Sigma
, so we deduce that
\int_{\Sigma}F_{\psi}^{\pm}=0
and then
(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm})^{-1}(F_{\psi}^{\pm})
is well-defined. Now we can resolve the traces
q^{\pm}|_{\Sigma}
from the evolution equations of
\psi
. We have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}(q^{+}|_{\Sigma})+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}(q^{-}|_{\Sigma})=\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)-F_{\psi}^{+}+F_{\psi}^{-}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\mp\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\mp}(\left\llbracket q\right\rrbracket|_{\Sigma})+\left(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\right)(q^{\pm}|_{\Sigma})=\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)-F_{\psi}^{+}+F_{\psi}^{-}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle~q^{\pm}|_{\Sigma}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left(\pm\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\mp}(\sigma\mathcal{H}(\psi))+\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)-\left\llbracket F_{\psi}\right\rrbracket\right),

(5.20)

where
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}:=\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}
(and equivalently we have
\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}\pm(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}))
represents the mixed Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator and
\left\llbracket F_{\psi}\right\rrbracket:=F_{\psi}^{+}-F_{\psi}^{-}
.

Plugging (
5.20
) back into the evolution equation of the free interface, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\rho^{+}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)=

\displaystyle~\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}(q^{+}|_{\Sigma})+F_{\psi}^{+}=\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}(\sigma\mathcal{H}(\psi))+\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)-F_{\psi}^{+}+F_{\psi}^{-}\right)+F_{\psi}^{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sigma\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}(\mathcal{H}(\psi))+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}F_{\psi}^{-}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}F_{\psi}^{+}+F_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sigma\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}(\mathcal{H}(\psi))+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}F_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}F_{\psi}^{-}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)\right).

(5.21)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\rho^{-}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)=\sigma\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}(\mathcal{H}(\psi))+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}F_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}F_{\psi}^{-}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)\right).

(5.22)

Now, using the expressions of
\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}
in terms of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}
and
\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}f=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}f+(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})f)=~\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}f+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})f,

(5.23)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}f=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}f-(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})f)=~\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}f-\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})f,

(5.24)

and also for
g^{\pm}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}
with
\int_{\Sigma}g^{\pm}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}g^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}g^{-}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}-(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}))\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}g^{+}+\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}+(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}))\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}g^{-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{g^{+}+g^{-}}{2}-\frac{1}{2}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left\llbracket g\right\rrbracket.

(5.25)

Let
f=\mathcal{H}(\psi)
and
g^{\pm}=F_{\psi}^{\pm}
in (
5.23
)-(
5.25
). We find that
\eqref{psi equ +}+\eqref{psi equ -}
can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\rho^{+}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)+\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\rho^{-}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)=

\displaystyle~\frac{\sigma}{2}\left(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\right)(\mathcal{H}(\psi))+F_{\psi}^{+}+F_{\psi}^{-}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sigma}{2}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})(\mathcal{H}(\psi))

\displaystyle-(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(F_{\psi}^{+}-F_{\psi}^{-})+(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)\right).

(5.26)

Recall that
F_{\psi}^{\pm}=\mathfrak{F}_{\psi}^{\pm}-(\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)_{\Sigma}
and
\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi=\mathcal{P}_{\neq 0}(\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)+(\rho^{\pm}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)_{\Sigma}
where

\mathfrak{F}_{\psi}^{\pm}:=-N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}+\left(\bar{b}_{i}^{\pm}\bar{b}_{j}^{\pm}-\rho^{\pm}\bar{v}_{i}^{\pm}\bar{v}_{j}^{\pm}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi-2(\rho^{\pm}\bar{v}^{\pm}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}\psi.

Thus, the evolution equation of the free interface becomes

\displaystyle(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\partial_{t}^{2}\psi=

\displaystyle~\frac{\sigma}{2}\left(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\right)(\mathcal{H}(\psi))+\left(\bar{b}_{i}^{+}\bar{b}_{j}^{+}-\rho^{+}\bar{v}_{i}^{+}\bar{v}_{j}^{+}+\bar{b}_{i}^{-}\bar{b}_{j}^{-}-\rho^{-}\bar{v}_{i}^{-}\bar{v}_{j}^{-}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi-2(\rho^{+}\bar{v}_{i}^{+}+\rho^{-}\bar{v}_{i}^{-})\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}\psi

\displaystyle-N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{+}-N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{-}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sigma}{2}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})(\mathcal{H}(\psi))-(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left(\left\llbracket\mathfrak{F}_{\psi}-\rho\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right\rrbracket\right),

(5.27)

where the first line is expected to give the
\sqrt{\sigma}
-weighted regularity (contributed by surface tension) and the non-weighted regularity (provided that stability condition (
1.27
)) for the free interface, the second line will be converted to the interior estimate of the right side of (
5.15
), and the last line consists of remainder terms that can be directly controlled by using paradifferential calculus.

5.2.2
Preliminaries on paradifferential calculus

In the equation (
5.27
), the term
\left(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\right)(\mathcal{H}(\psi))
is a fully nonlinear term. Although it is well-known that the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator is a first-order elliptic operator and the mean-curvature operator is a second-order elliptic operator, it is still necessary for us to find out their concrete forms and “symmetrize” the paradifferential formulations in order for an explicit energy estimate. In this subsection, we introduce several preliminary lemmas about paradifferential calculus that have been proven in Alazard-Burq-Zuily
[
2
]
. Following the notations in Métivier
[
38
]
, we first introduce the basic definition of a paradifferential operator. Note that the dimension
d
below is not the same as the one in Section 1.

Definition 5.2

(Symbols)
.

Given
r\geq 0,~m\in\mathbb{R}
, we denote
\Gamma_{r}^{m}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
to be the space of locally bounded functions
a(x^{\prime},\xi)
on
\mathbb{T}^{d}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{0\})
, which are
C^{\infty}
with respect to
\xi(\xi\neq\mathbf{0})
, such that for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d},\xi\neq\mathbf{0}
, the function
x^{\prime}\mapsto\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}a(x^{\prime},\xi)
belongs to
W^{r,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and there exists a constant
C_{\alpha}
such that

\left|\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}a(\cdot,\xi)\right|_{W^{r,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\leq C_{\alpha}(1+|\xi|)^{m-|\alpha|},~~\forall|\xi|\geq 1/2.

Definition 5.3

(Paradifferential operator)
.

Given a symbol
a
, we shall define the
paradifferential operator
T_{a}
by

\displaystyle\widehat{T_{a}u}(\xi):=(2\pi)^{-d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\tilde{\chi}(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{a}(\xi-\eta,\eta)\phi(\eta)\hat{u}(\eta)\,\mathrm{d}\eta

(5.28)

where
\hat{a}(\theta,\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\exp(-ix^{\prime}\cdot\theta)a(x^{\prime},\xi)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}
is the Fourier transform of
a
in variable
x^{\prime}
. Here
\tilde{\chi}
and
\phi
are two given cut-off functions such that

\phi(\eta)=0~~\text{for }|\eta|\leq 1,\quad\phi(\eta)=1~~\text{for }|\eta|\geq 2,

and
\tilde{\chi}(\theta,\eta)
is homogeneous of degree 0 and satisfies that for
0<\varepsilon_{1}<\varepsilon_{2}\ll 1
,
\tilde{\chi}(\theta,\eta)=1
if
|\theta|\leq\varepsilon_{1}|\eta|
and
\tilde{\chi}(\theta,\eta)=0
if
|\theta|\geq\varepsilon_{2}|\eta|
. We also introduce the semi-norm

\displaystyle M_{r}^{a}(a):=\sup_{|\alpha|\leq\frac{d}{2}+1+r}\sup_{|\xi|\geq 1/2}\left|(1+|\xi|)^{|\alpha|-m}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}a(\cdot,\xi)\right|_{W^{r,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}.

(5.29)

For
m\in\mathbb{R}
, we say
T
is of order
m
if for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
T
is bounded from
H^{s}
to
H^{s-m}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{R}
. If
a\in\Gamma_{0}^{m}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, then
T_{a}
is of order
m
. Moreover, for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists a constant
K
such that
\|T_{a}\|_{H^{s}\to H^{s-m}}\leq KM_{0}^{m}(a)
.

Proposition 5.2

(Composition,
[
2
, Theorem 3.7]
)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{R}
and
r>0
. If
a\in\Gamma_{r}^{m}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),~b\in\Gamma_{r}^{m^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, then
T_{a}T_{b}-T_{a\#b}
is of order
m+m^{\prime}-r
where

a\#b:=\sum_{|\alpha|<r}\frac{1}{i^{|\alpha|}\alpha!}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}a\partial_{x^{\prime}}^{\alpha}b.

Moreover, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists a constant
K
such that

\displaystyle\|T_{a}T_{b}-T_{a\#b}\|_{H^{s}\to H^{s-m-m^{\prime}+r}}\leq KM_{r}^{m}(a)M_{r}^{m^{\prime}}(b).

(5.30)

Proposition 5.3

(Adjoint,
[
2
, Theorem 3.10]
)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{R}
,
r>0
and
a\in\Gamma_{r}^{m}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
. We denote by
(T_{a})^{*}
the adjoint operator of
T_{a}
. Then
(T_{a})^{*}-T_{a^{*}}
is of order
m-r
where

a^{*}:=\sum_{|\alpha|<r}\frac{1}{i^{\alpha}\alpha!}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}\partial_{x^{\prime}}^{\alpha}\bar{a}.

Moreover, for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists a constant
K
such that
\|(T_{a})^{*}-T_{a^{*}}\|_{H^{s}\to H^{s-m+r}}\leq KM_{r}^{m}(a)
.

The symbolic calculus adopted in this paper is not of
C^{\infty}
-regularity. We shall introduce the following class of symbols. Here and thereafter in this section,
