\psi\in C([0,T];H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T}^{d}))
is a given function with
s>2+\frac{d}{2}
.

Definition 5.4
.

Given
m\in\mathbb{R}
, we denote
\Sigma^{m}
to be the class of symbols
a
of the form
a=a^{(m)}+a^{(m-1)}
with

a^{(m)}(t,x^{\prime},\xi)=F(\overline{\nabla}_{x^{\prime}}\psi(t,x^{\prime}),\xi),\quad a^{(m-1)}(t,x^{\prime},\xi)=\sum_{|\alpha|_{2}}G_{\alpha}(\overline{\nabla}_{x^{\prime}}\psi(t,x^{\prime}),\xi)\partial_{x^{\prime}}^{\alpha}\psi(t,x^{\prime})

such that

i.

T_{a}
maps real-valued functions to real-valued functions;

ii.

F
is a
C^{\infty}
real-valued functions of
(\zeta,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{0\})
, homogeneous of degree
m
in
\xi
, such that there exists a continuous function
K=K(\zeta)>0
such that
F(\zeta,\xi)\geq K(\zeta)|\xi|^{m}
for all
(\zeta,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{0\})
;

iii.

G_{\alpha}
is a
C^{\infty}
complex-valued function of
(\zeta,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{0\})
, homogeneous of degree
m-1
in
\xi
.

Definition 5.5

(“Equivalence” of operators)
.

Given
m\in\mathbb{R}
and consider two families of operators of order
m
:
\{A(t):t\in[0,T]\}
and
\{B(t):t\in[0,T]\}
, We say
A\sim B
if
A-B
is of order
m-1.5
and satisfies the estimate: for all
r\in\mathbb{R}
there exists a continuous function
C(\cdot)
such that

\forall t\in[0,T],~~\|A(t)-B(t)\|_{H^{r}\to H^{r-(m-1.5)}}\leq C(|\psi(t)|_{s+\frac{1}{2}}).

From now on, we use the notation
|\cdot|_{s_{1}\to s_{2}}
to represent the operator norm
\|\cdot\|_{H^{s_{1}}\to H^{s_{2}}}
, and use the notation
|\cdot|_{s}
to represent
\|\cdot\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}
, as we only apply paradifferential calculus on the free interface
\Sigma
. With this definition, we have

Proposition 5.4

(
[
2
, Prop. 4.3]
)
.

Let
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

1.

If
a\in\Sigma^{m},~b\in\Sigma^{m^{\prime}}
, then
T_{a}T_{b}\sim T_{a\#b}
where
a\#b
is given by

a\#b=a^{(m)}b^{(m^{\prime})}+a^{(m-1)}b^{(m^{\prime})}+a^{(m)}b^{(m^{\prime}-1)}+\frac{1}{i}\partial_{\xi}a^{(m)}\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}b^{(m^{\prime})}.

2.

If
a\in\Sigma^{m}
, then
(T_{a})^{*}\sim T_{b}
where
b\in\Sigma^{m}
is given by

b=a^{(m)}+\overline{a^{(m-1)}}+\frac{1}{i}(\partial_{x^{\prime}}\cdot\partial_{\xi})a^{(m)}.

As a corollary, we have

Corollary 5.5

(
[
2
, Prop. 4.3(2)]
)
.

If
a\in\Sigma^{m}
satisfies
\textbf{Im }a^{(m-1)}=-0.5(\partial_{\xi}\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}})a^{(m)}
, then
(T_{a})^{*}\sim T_{a}
.

The next proposition is significant for the estimate of Sobolev norms via paradifferential calculus.

Proposition 5.6

(
[
2
, Prop. 4.4 and 4.6]
)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{R},~r\in\mathbb{R}
. Then for all symbol
a\in\Sigma^{m}
and
t\in[0,T]
, the following estimate holds.

\displaystyle|T_{a(t)}u|_{r-m}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\psi(t)|_{s-1})|u|_{r},

(5.31)

\displaystyle|u|_{r+m}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\psi(t)|_{s-1})\left(|T_{a(t)}u|_{r}+|u|_{0}\right).

(5.32)

5.2.3
Paralinearization of the nonlinear terms

Now we can start to paralinearize the term
\left(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\right)(\mathcal{H}(\psi))
in (
5.27
).

Lemma 5.7

(Paralinearization of the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator,
[
4
, Section 4.4]
)
.

For
f,\psi\in H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}f=T_{\Lambda^{\pm}}\psi+R_{\Lambda,1}^{\pm}(\psi,f)+R_{\Lambda,2}^{\pm}(\psi,f),

(5.33)

with the symbols
\lambda^{\pm}=\lambda^{(1),\pm}+\lambda^{(0),\pm}
give by

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(1),\pm}=

\displaystyle\sqrt{(1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2})|\xi|^{2}-(\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\xi)^{2}},

(5.34)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(0),-}=-\overline{\Lambda^{(0),+}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}|}{2\Lambda^{(1),-}}\left(\overline{\nabla}\cdot(\alpha^{(1)}\overline{\nabla}\psi)+i\partial_{\xi}\Lambda^{(1),-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\alpha^{(1)}\right),

(5.35)

and
\alpha^{(1)}:=(\Lambda^{(1),-}+i\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\xi)/(1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2})
. The remainder terms satisfy the following estimates

\displaystyle|R_{\Lambda,1}^{\pm}(\psi,f)|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(|\psi|_{C^{2}},|f|_{3})|f|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},\quad|R_{\Lambda,2}^{\pm}(\psi,f)|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(|\psi|_{s-\frac{1}{2}})|\overline{\partial}{}f|_{s-2}.

(5.36)

Lemma 5.8

(Paralinearization of the mean curvature operator,
[
2
, Lemma 3.25]
)
.

There holds
\mathcal{H}(\psi)=-T_{\mathfrak{H}}f+R_{\mathfrak{H}}
where
\mathfrak{H}=\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}+\mathfrak{H}^{(1)}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}\left(|\xi|^{2}-\frac{(\overline{\nabla}\psi\cdot\xi)^{2}}{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}\right),

(5.37)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}(\overline{\nabla}_{x^{\prime}}\cdot\partial_{\xi})\mathfrak{H}^{(2)},

(5.38)

and the remainder term
R_{\mathfrak{H}}
satisfies

\displaystyle|R_{\mathfrak{H}}|_{2s-3}\leq C(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}}).

(5.39)

With the paralinearization of operators
\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}
and
\mathcal{H}(\psi)
, the term
\left(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\right)(\mathcal{H}(\psi))
in (
5.27
) becomes

\displaystyle\sigma\left(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-}\right)(\mathcal{H}(\psi))=-\sigma T_{\Lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}\psi+\sigma\mathcal{R}_{\psi}^{\sigma},

(5.40)

where
-\overline{\Lambda^{(0),+}}=\Lambda^{(0),-}
shows that
\textbf{Re }(\Lambda^{(0),+})+\textbf{Re }(\Lambda^{(0),-})=0
,
\textbf{Im }(\Lambda^{(0),+})=\textbf{Im }(\Lambda^{(0),+})
and thus

\displaystyle\Lambda:=

\displaystyle~\underbrace{(\Lambda^{(1),+}+\Lambda^{(1),-})}_{=:\Lambda^{(1)}}+\underbrace{(\Lambda^{(0),+}+\Lambda^{(0),-})}_{=:\Lambda^{(0)}}=2\Lambda^{(1),-}+2i\textbf{Im }(\Lambda^{(0),-})

(5.41)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\psi}^{\sigma}:=

\displaystyle\sum_{\pm}T_{\Lambda^{\pm}}R_{\mathfrak{H}}+R_{\Lambda,1}^{\pm}(\psi,\mathcal{H}(\psi))+R_{\Lambda,2}^{\pm}(\psi,\mathcal{H}(\psi))\text{ and }|\mathcal{R}_{\psi}^{\sigma}|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}})|\psi|_{s+1}.

(5.42)

In order for an explicit energy estimate for
\psi
and
\psi_{t}
, we shall symmetrize the 3-rd order paradifferential operator
T_{\Lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}
. That is, find suitable symbols
{\mathfrak{m}}\in\Sigma^{1.5}
and
{\mathfrak{n}}\in\Sigma^{0}
such that
T_{\mathfrak{n}}T_{\Lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}\sim T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}
and
T_{\mathfrak{m}}\sim(T_{\mathfrak{m}})^{*}
.

Proposition 5.9

(Symmetrisation of the composition)
.

Let
{\mathfrak{n}}\in\Sigma^{0}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}\in\Sigma^{1.5}
be defined by

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{n}}:=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{\sqrt[3]{2}\sqrt[4]{1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2}}}=2^{-\frac{1}{3}}|N|^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(5.43)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{m}}:=

\displaystyle~\underbrace{\sqrt{\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}\Lambda^{(1)}}}_{=:{\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2i}(\partial_{\xi}\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}})\sqrt{\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}\Lambda^{(1)}}}_{=:{\mathfrak{m}}^{(0.5)}}.

(5.44)

Then
T_{\mathfrak{n}}T_{\lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}\sim T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}
and
T_{\mathfrak{m}}\sim(T_{\mathfrak{m}})^{*}
are both fulfilled.

Proof.

Given the symbol
\Lambda
and
\mathfrak{H}
, we shall find suitable symbols
{\mathfrak{n}}\in\Sigma^{0},{\mathfrak{m}}\in\Sigma^{1.5}
such that
{\mathfrak{n}}(x^{\prime},\xi)
is independent of
\xi
and
{\mathfrak{n}}\#(\Lambda\#\mathfrak{H})=({\mathfrak{m}}\#{\mathfrak{m}})\#{\mathfrak{n}}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle~{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}(\Lambda\#\mathfrak{H})+{\mathfrak{n}}^{(-1)}\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{i}\partial_{\xi}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}(\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~({\mathfrak{m}}\#{\mathfrak{m}}){\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}+({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)})^{2}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(-1)}+\frac{1}{i}\partial_{\xi}(({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)})^{2})\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}.

Recall that

\displaystyle(\Lambda\#\mathfrak{H})=

\displaystyle~\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}+\Lambda^{(0)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}+\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{i}\partial_{\xi}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}\Lambda^{(1)},

\displaystyle({\mathfrak{m}}\#{\mathfrak{m}})=

\displaystyle~({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)})^{2}+2({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)})({\mathfrak{m}}^{(0.5)})+\frac{1}{i}\partial_{\xi}({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)})\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)}).

We choose the principal symbol
{\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)}:=\sqrt{\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}}
in order for cancelling the leading-order symbols. Since we require
(T_{{\mathfrak{m}}})^{*}\sim T_{{\mathfrak{m}}^{*}}
, we must have
\textbf{Im }({\mathfrak{m}}^{(0.5)})=-0.5(\partial_{x^{\prime}}\cdot\partial_{\xi}){\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)}
(cf.
[
2
, Prop. 4.3]
). With this choice for
{\mathfrak{m}}
, it remains to solve the symbolic equation

{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}(\Lambda\#\mathfrak{H}-{\mathfrak{m}}\#{\mathfrak{m}})=\frac{1}{i}\partial_{\xi}((\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)})\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}-\frac{1}{i}\partial_{x^{\prime}}(\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)})\cdot\partial_{\xi}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)},

(5.45)

with

\displaystyle\Lambda\#\mathfrak{H}-{\mathfrak{m}}\#{\mathfrak{m}}=\Lambda^{(0)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}+\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(1)}-2({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)})({\mathfrak{m}}^{(0.5)})+\frac{1}{i}\partial_{\xi}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}\Lambda^{(1)}-\frac{1}{i}\partial_{\xi}({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)})\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)}).

The sub-principle
{\mathfrak{n}}^{(-1)}
does not appear, so we can choose
{\mathfrak{n}}^{(-1)}=0
. Since the principal symbols of
\Lambda
and
\mathfrak{H}
are real-valued, we now just need to solve

\textbf{Re }(\Lambda\#\mathfrak{H}-{\mathfrak{m}}\#{\mathfrak{m}})=0,~~{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}\textbf{Im }(\Lambda\#\mathfrak{H}-{\mathfrak{m}}\#{\mathfrak{m}})=-\partial_{\xi}((\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)})\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}+\partial_{x^{\prime}}(\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)})\cdot\partial_{\xi}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}.

The condition for the real part is fulfilled if we have

\underbrace{\textbf{Re }(\Lambda^{(0)})}_{=0}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}=2{\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)}\textbf{Re }({\mathfrak{m}}^{(0.5)})\Rightarrow\textbf{Re }({\mathfrak{m}}^{(0.5)})=0.

For the imaginary part, inserting the symbols
\mathfrak{H}^{(1)},~\textbf{Im }(\Lambda^{(0)}),~{\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)}
and
\textbf{Im }({\mathfrak{m}}^{(0.5)})
, we get

\displaystyle\textbf{Im }(\Lambda\#\mathfrak{H}-{\mathfrak{m}}\#{\mathfrak{m}})=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\xi}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}\Lambda^{(1)}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x^{\prime}}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}\cdot\partial_{\xi}\Lambda^{(1)},

and thus we need to solve

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}\left(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\xi}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}\Lambda^{(1)}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{x^{\prime}}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}\cdot\partial_{\xi}\Lambda^{(1)}\right)=-\partial_{\xi}((\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)})\cdot\partial_{x^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}+\partial_{x^{\prime}}(\Lambda^{(1)}\mathfrak{H}^{(2)})\cdot\partial_{\xi}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}.

(5.46)

Notice that
\mathfrak{H}^{(2)}=(c\Lambda^{(1)})^{2}
with
c=\frac{1}{2}(1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2})^{-\frac{3}{4}}
. Plugging it to the above equation, after a long and tedious calculation, we get the following relation

{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}\left(c^{2}\partial_{x^{\prime}}\Lambda^{(1)}(\Lambda^{(1)})-(\partial_{x^{\prime}}c)c(\Lambda^{(1)})^{2}-c^{2}\partial_{x^{\prime}}(\Lambda^{(1)})\Lambda^{(1)}\right)=-3c^{2}(\Lambda^{(1)})^{2}\partial_{x^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)},

that is,

\frac{\partial_{x^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}}{{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}}=\frac{-(\partial_{x^{\prime}}c)c(\Lambda^{(1)})^{2}}{-3c^{2}(\Lambda^{(1)})^{2}}=\frac{1}{3}\frac{\partial_{x^{\prime}}c}{c}\Rightarrow{\mathfrak{n}}^{(0)}=c^{\frac{1}{3}}=2^{-\frac{1}{3}}(1+|\overline{\nabla}\psi|^{2})^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

∎

We expect to take
(s-\frac{1}{2})
-th order derivatives in (
5.27
). In view of the paradifferential formulation, we shall alternatively take
T_{{\mathfrak{M}}}
with

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}}:=({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)})^{\frac{2s-1}{3}}=2^{\frac{2s-1}{6}}|\xi|^{s-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1-\left|\frac{N}{|N|}\cdot\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{s}{2}-\frac{1}{4}}\in\Sigma^{s-\frac{1}{2}}

(5.47)

for sake of simplicity. Below, we list several commutator estimates for the paradifferential operators.

Lemma 5.10
.

For any
r\in\mathbb{R},~s>2+\frac{d}{2}
, any functions
a
and
f
, the following commutator estimates hold

\displaystyle\left|\left[T_{\mathfrak{M}},T_{\mathfrak{m}}\right]\right|_{r+s-1\to r}\leq

\displaystyle~C\left(|\psi|_{s+0.5}\right),

\displaystyle\left|[T_{\mathfrak{n}},T_{a}]f\right|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})|a|_{W^{1,\infty}}|f|_{s-1.5},

\displaystyle\left|\left[T_{\mathfrak{M}},a\right]T_{\mathfrak{n}}f\right|_{0}+\left|T_{\mathfrak{M}}\left[T_{\mathfrak{n}},a\right]f\right|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})|a|_{s-0.5}|f|_{s-1.5},

\displaystyle\left|[T_{\mathfrak{m}},a]f\right|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})|a|_{s-0.5}|f|_{0.5}.

We also need to commute
\partial_{t}
with paradifferential operators. These steps will generate paradifferential operators whose symbols are spatial or time derivatives.

Lemma 5.11
.

For any
r\in\mathbb{R}
, the following estimates hold

\displaystyle|T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{n}}}|_{r\to r}+|T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{M}}}|_{r\to r-(s-0.5)}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}},|\partial_{t}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}),

\displaystyle|T_{\partial_{t}^{2}{\mathfrak{n}}}|_{r\to r}+|T_{\partial_{t}^{2}{\mathfrak{M}}}|_{r\to r-(s-0.5)}\leq

\displaystyle~C\left(\left|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\partial_{t}\psi,\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right),

\displaystyle|T_{\overline{\partial}{}{\mathfrak{n}}}|_{r\to r}+|T_{\overline{\partial}{}{\mathfrak{M}}}|_{r\to r-(s-0.5)}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}),

\displaystyle|T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{m}}}|_{r\to r-1.5}+|T_{\overline{\partial}{}{\mathfrak{m}}}|_{r\to r-1.5}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}},|\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}}).

5.3
Uniform estimates for the free interface

With the symmetrized paralinearization of
(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}+\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\mathcal{H}(\psi)
derived in Section
5.2.2
, we can now prove the uniform-in-
({\varepsilon,\sigma})
estimates of
\psi
under the stability condition (
1.27
). Our desired result is listed in the following proposition.

Proposition 5.12
.

For a fixed
\sigma>0
, there is a time
T_{\sigma}>0
(independent of
\varepsilon
) such that the following uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimates hold true

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi\right|_{5}^{2}+\left|\psi_{t}\right|_{3.5}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi_{t}\right|_{4}^{2}+\left|\psi_{tt}\right|_{2.5}^{2}\right)\lesssim\sigma^{-1}P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t))E_{5}(t).

(5.48)

Moreover, if the stability condition (
1.27
) is fulfilled, then there exists a time
T>0
(independent of
{\varepsilon,\sigma}
) such that the following uniform-in-
({\varepsilon,\sigma})
estimates hold true

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi\right|_{5}^{2}+\left|\psi_{t}\right|_{3.5}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi_{t}\right|_{4}^{2}+\left|\psi_{tt}\right|_{2.5}^{2}\right)\lesssim P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t))\widetilde{E}_{5}(t).

(5.49)

Step 1: Find equivalent energy functionals

The equation (
5.27
) can be written as

\displaystyle(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\partial_{t}^{2}\psi=

\displaystyle~-\frac{\sigma}{2}T_{\Lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}\psi-(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})(\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j})\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi-2(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}\psi

\displaystyle+\left(\rho^{+}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{i}^{+}\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{j}^{+}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{j})+\rho^{-}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{i}^{-}\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{j}^{-}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{j})\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi

\displaystyle-(N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{+}+N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{-})+\Psi^{R},

(5.50)

where
T_{\Lambda},T_{\mathfrak{H}}
are the paradifferential operators defined in Proposition
5.7
and Proposition
5.8
, the quantities
\mathbf{w},\mathbf{u},\mathbf{b}
are defined by

\displaystyle\mathbf{w}:=\frac{\rho^{+}v^{+}+\rho^{-}v^{-}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}},\quad\mathbf{u}:=\frac{\sqrt{\rho^{+}\rho^{-}}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}\left\llbracket v\right\rrbracket,\quad\mathbf{b}^{\pm}:=\frac{b^{\pm}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\pm}}},

(5.51)

and
\Psi^{R}
is defined by

\displaystyle\Psi^{R}:=\frac{\sigma}{2}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})(\mathcal{H}(\psi))-(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left(\left\llbracket\mathfrak{F}_{\psi}-\rho\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right\rrbracket\right)+\frac{\sigma}{2}\mathcal{R}_{\psi}^{\sigma}.

(5.52)

Note that
\mathcal{R}_{\psi}^{\sigma}
has been defined in (
5.42
).

We pick
s=4
in the paradifferential operator
T_{\mathfrak{M}}
, that is,
{\mathfrak{M}}=({\mathfrak{m}}^{(1.5)})^{\frac{7}{3}}\in\Sigma^{3.5}
and then consider the energy functionals

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t):=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\left|(\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma}\left|\sqrt{\sigma}T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|_{0}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(5.53)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t):=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\rho^{+}\left(\left|\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\overline{\nabla}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}-\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\overline{\nabla}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}\right)+\rho^{-}\left(\left|\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\overline{\nabla}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}-\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\overline{\nabla}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(5.54)

Lemma 5.13

(Comparison between
\mathcal{E},\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
and Sobolev norms)
.

For any fixed
\sigma>0
, we have the following relations between
\mathcal{E},\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
and standard Sobolev norms.

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi\right|_{5}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\mathcal{E}(t)+|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi|_{0}^{2}\right),

\displaystyle|\psi|_{4.5}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle~|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi|_{5}^{2}+\sigma^{-1}|\psi|_{0}^{2},

\displaystyle|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t},\bar{v}^{\pm},\rho^{\pm}|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\mathcal{E}(t)+|\psi|_{4.5}^{2}+|\psi_{t}|_{0}^{2}\right)

where
C(\cdot)
represents a generic positive continuous function in its arguments. Moreover, when the stability condition (
1.27
) holds, there exist positive continuous functions
C_{1},C_{1}^{\prime},C_{1}^{\prime\prime}
depending on
|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\bar{v}^{\pm},\bar{b}^{\pm},\rho^{\pm}|_{W^{1,\infty}}
and independent of
\sigma
, such that

\displaystyle C_{1}\left(|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\bar{v}^{\pm},\bar{b}^{\pm},\rho^{\pm}|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left|\psi\right|_{4.5}^{2}\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t)+C_{1}^{\prime}|\psi|_{0}^{2},~~\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t)\leq C_{1}^{\prime\prime}\left(|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\bar{v}^{\pm},\bar{b}^{\pm},\rho^{\pm}|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)\left|\psi\right|_{4.5}^{2}.

Proof.

Recall that
T_{\mathfrak{M}}
and
T_{\mathfrak{m}}
are paradifferential operators of order 3.5 and 1.5 respectively, thus the first inequality is a direct consequence of Proposition
5.6
. The second inequality is a directly consequence of Sobolev interpolation and Young’s inequality

|\psi|_{4.5}^{2}\leq|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi|_{5}^{1.8}|\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\psi|_{0}^{0.2}\leq\frac{|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi|_{5}^{2}}{10/9}+\frac{|\psi|_{0}^{2}}{10\sigma}.

To prove the third inequality, we again use Proposition
5.6
to get

\displaystyle|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(|T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi_{t}|_{0}^{2}+|\psi_{t}|_{0}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\bar{v}^{\pm},\rho^{\pm}|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\mathcal{E}(t)+|T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{n}}}\psi|_{0}^{2}+|T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi|_{0}^{2}+|\psi_{t}|_{0}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t},\bar{v}^{\pm},\rho^{\pm}|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\mathcal{E}(t)+|\psi|_{3.5}^{2}+|\psi_{t}|_{0}^{2}\right).

For the last inequality in this lemma, the right side is trivial. When the stability condition (
1.27
) holds, it suffices to prove that
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t)
is a positive-definite energy, then the left side automatically holds. Multiplying
(\rho^{+}\rho^{-})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in (
1.27
), the stability condition becomes

\displaystyle\exists\delta_{0}\in(0,\frac{1}{8}),\quad|\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\times\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}|\geq(1-\delta_{0})^{-1}|\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}\times\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket|\sqrt{1+(c_{A}^{\pm}/c_{s}^{\pm})^{2}}>(1-\delta_{0})^{-1}|\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{\pm}\times\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket|.

(5.55)

Since
\left\llbracket v\right\rrbracket\cdot N=0
and
\mathbf{b}^{\pm}
are nonzero and not collinear, we may assume
\left\llbracket v\right\rrbracket=c_{1}\mathbf{b}^{+}+c_{2}\mathbf{b}^{-}
. Plugging this into the stability condition, we get
c_{1},c_{2}\leq 1-\delta_{0}
. Using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we derive that

\displaystyle\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\\
|\mathbf{z}|=1\end{subarray}}(1-\delta_{0})^{2}\left((\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}+2(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})+(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}\right)-\left(\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket\cdot\mathbf{z}\right)^{2}\geq 0

Invoking
\bar{\mathbf{u}}=\frac{\sqrt{\rho^{+}\rho^{-}}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}\left\llbracket\bar{v}\right\rrbracket
and using the non-collinearity, the above inequality implies that

\displaystyle~\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\\
|\mathbf{z}|=1\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\rho^{+}\rho^{-}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}+2\frac{\rho^{+}\rho^{-}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})+\frac{\rho^{+}\rho^{-}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}-(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\mathbf{z}\right)^{2}\right)>0.

Notice that

\displaystyle\rho^{+}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}+\rho^{-}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}-\left(\frac{\rho^{+}\rho^{-}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}+2\frac{\rho^{+}\rho^{-}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})+\frac{\rho^{+}\rho^{-}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}\left((\rho^{+})^{2}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}-2\rho^{+}\rho^{-}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})+(\rho^{-})^{2}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}\right)\geq 0.

Thus, it implies that

\displaystyle~\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\\
|\mathbf{z}|=1\end{subarray}}\left(\rho^{+}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}+\rho^{-}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}-(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\mathbf{z}\right)^{2}\right)>0,

(5.56)

or equivalently, there exists some
\delta_{0}^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle~\inf_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(\rho^{+}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{+}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}+\rho^{-}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z})^{2}-(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\mathbf{z}\right)^{2}\right)\geq 2\delta_{0}^{\prime}|\mathbf{z}|^{2}.

(5.57)

Now let
\mathbf{z}=\overline{\nabla}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi
, the above inequality shows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t)\geq\delta_{0}^{\prime}|\overline{\nabla}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi|_{0}^{2}\gtrsim|\psi|_{4.5}^{2}-|\psi|_{0}^{2}
.
∎

Remark 5.1

(The 2D case)
.

When the space dimension
d=2
, we no longer have the non-collinearity, but the stability condition (
1.35
) still guarantees the ellipticity of the corresponding second-order differential operator, i.e.,

\exists\delta_{0}^{\prime}>0,\quad\rho^{+}\left((\mathbf{b}_{1}^{+})^{2}-\mathbf{u}_{1}^{2}\right)+\rho^{-}\left((\mathbf{b}_{1}^{-})^{2}-\mathbf{u}_{1}^{2}\right)\geq\delta_{0}^{\prime}.

In fact, the stability condition (
1.35
) implies
|\mathbf{b}_{1}^{+}|+|\mathbf{b}_{1}^{-}|\geq(1+\delta_{0})|\left\llbracket v_{1}\right\rrbracket|
. Taking square and invoking
\mathbf{u}:=\frac{\sqrt{\rho^{+}\rho^{-}}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}\left\llbracket v\right\rrbracket
, we get

\frac{\rho^{+}\rho^{-}}{\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}}\left((\mathbf{b}_{1}^{+})^{2}+2\mathbf{b}_{1}^{+}\mathbf{b}_{1}^{-}+(\mathbf{b}_{1}^{-})^{2}\right)\geq(1+\delta_{0})(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\mathbf{u}_{1}^{2},

in which we find that the left side does not exceed
(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})^{-1}\left(\rho^{+}(\mathbf{b}_{1}^{+})^{2}+\rho^{-}(\mathbf{b}_{1}^{-})^{2}\right)
by direct calculation. The desired result immediately follows thanks to
|\mathbf{u}_{1}|>0
(otherwise the interface is not a vortex sheet).

Step 2: Control of
|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}
and
|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi|_{5}

In view of Lemma
5.13
, it suffices to prove energy estimates for
\mathcal{E}(t)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t)
under the stability condition (
1.27
). We start with the estimate of
|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}
.

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\left|(\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})(\partial_{t}^{2}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi)\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\int_{\Sigma}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})(\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla}\partial_{t}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi)\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})(\partial_{t}\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi)\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\left|(\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle~I_{0}+I_{1}+I_{1}^{R}+I_{2}^{R}.

(5.58)

The remainder terms are easy to control. Using Proposition
5.6
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}^{R}+I_{2}^{R}\leq C\left(\left|\rho^{\pm},\partial_{t}\rho^{\pm},\bar{v}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\bar{v}^{\pm}\right|_{L^{\infty}},|\psi|_{3}\right)\left(|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}^{2}+|\psi|_{4.5}^{2}\right).

(5.59)

For the main term
I_{0}
, we first commute
(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\partial_{t}^{2}
with
T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}

\displaystyle(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\partial_{t}^{2}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi=

\displaystyle~(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\left(T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi+(T_{\partial_{t}^{2}{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}+T_{{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{\partial_{t}^{2}{\mathfrak{n}}})\psi+2(T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{{\mathfrak{n}}}+T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{n}}})\partial_{t}\psi\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\left((\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right)-\left([T_{\mathfrak{M}},\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}]T_{\mathfrak{n}}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi+T_{\mathfrak{M}}([T_{\mathfrak{n}},\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}]\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)\right)

\displaystyle+(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\left((T_{\partial_{t}^{2}{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}+T_{{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{\partial_{t}^{2}{\mathfrak{n}}})\psi+2(T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{{\mathfrak{n}}}+T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{n}}})\partial_{t}\psi\right).

The commutators can be controlled straightforwardly thanks to Lemma
5.10
and Lemma
5.11
:

\displaystyle\left|[T_{\mathfrak{M}},\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}]T_{\mathfrak{n}}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right|_{0}+\left|T_{\mathfrak{M}}([T_{\mathfrak{n}},\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}]\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)\right|_{0}\leq C(|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(|\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}|_{3.5}|\partial_{t}^{2}\psi|_{2.5}\right),

\displaystyle\left|(T_{\partial_{t}^{2}{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}+T_{{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{\partial_{t}^{2}{\mathfrak{n}}})\psi\right|_{0}+\left|(T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{{\mathfrak{n}}}+T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{n}}})\partial_{t}\psi\right|_{0}\leq C(|\psi_{tt},\psi_{t},\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(|\psi|_{3.5}+|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}\right).

In the remaining of this section, we no longer explicitly write the commutators between the paradifferential operators and functions or
\partial_{t},\overline{\partial}{}_{i}
, as they can be controlled in the same way as above. Instead, we will again use the notation
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}
to skip these terms and analyze the main terms.

Then we can invoke (
5.50
) to get

\displaystyle I_{00}:=

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}((\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)\right)\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}T_{\Lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}\psi\right)\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-2\int_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}((\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}\psi)\right)\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}((\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi)\right)\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\left(\rho^{+}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{i}^{+}\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{j}^{+}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{j})+\rho^{-}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{i}^{-}\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{j}^{-}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{j})\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi\right)\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\left(N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{+}+N\cdot q_{w}^{-}\right)\right)\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\Psi^{R}\right)\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}=:I_{000}+I_{001}+I_{002}+I_{003}+I_{0}^{w}+I_{0}^{R}.

(5.60)

Since Proposition
5.9
indicates that
T_{\mathfrak{n}}T_{\Lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}\sim T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}
and
(T_{\mathfrak{m}})^{*}\sim T_{\mathfrak{m}}
, we have

\displaystyle~T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}T_{\Lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}\psi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(T_{\mathfrak{m}})^{*}T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi+\big(((T_{\mathfrak{m}})^{*}-T_{\mathfrak{m}})T_{\mathfrak{M}}+T_{\mathfrak{m}}[T_{\mathfrak{M}},T_{\mathfrak{m}}]+[T_{\mathfrak{M}},T_{\mathfrak{m}}]T_{\mathfrak{m}}\big)T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}(T_{\mathfrak{m}})^{*}T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi

and using the duality, we get

\displaystyle I_{000}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi)\,T_{\mathfrak{m}}(\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma}{4}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}\left|T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{\sigma}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(T_{\mathfrak{m}}T_{{\mathfrak{M}}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi)\,\left(T_{\partial_{t}{\mathfrak{m}}}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{i}T_{\overline{\partial}{}_{i}{\mathfrak{m}}}+\frac{1}{2}(\overline{\nabla}\cdot\bar{\mathbf{w}})-[T_{{\mathfrak{m}}},\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{i}]\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\right)T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime},

(5.61)

where the second term can be directly controlled (uniformly in
\sigma
) by
\mathcal{E}(t)C\left(|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\psi_{t},\bar{v}^{\pm},\rho^{\pm}|_{W^{1,\infty}}\right)
thanks to Lemma
5.10
. Next we analyze
I_{1}+I_{001}
and
I_{002},I_{003}
. For a generic function
\mathbf{a}\in H^{3.5}(\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}^{2})
and a generic
\bm{\rho}\in H^{3.5}(\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}_{+})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\left(T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}(\bm{\rho}\mathbf{a}_{i}\mathbf{a}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi)\right)\,\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\bm{\rho}\mathbf{a}_{i}\mathbf{a}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\,\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\int_{\Sigma}\bm{\rho}(\mathbf{a}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi)\,(\mathbf{a}_{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}(\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}\bm{\rho}\left|\mathbf{a}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

Setting
\mathbf{a}=\bar{\mathbf{w}},\bar{\mathbf{u}},\bar{\mathbf{b}}
and
\bm{\rho}=\rho^{\pm}
or
\rho^{+}+\rho^{-}
, we immediately get

I_{002}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\left|(\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,\quad I_{003}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t).

For
I_{1}+I_{001}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{001}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-2\int_{\Sigma}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})(\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}T_{{\mathfrak{m}}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\,\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow I_{1}+I_{001}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})(\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})\partial_{t}T_{{\mathfrak{m}}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\,\left((\partial_{t}+\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right)\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{=}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Sigma}(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\left|(\bar{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\overline{\nabla})T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}\psi\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,

(5.62)

which cancels with the main term in
I_{002}
. When
\sigma>0
is given and the stability condition (
1.27
) is not assumed, the quantity
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t)
is not necessarily positive, but its contribution, namely the term
I_{003}
, can be controlled by integrating by parts for 1/2-derivative

I_{003}\lesssim P(\|v^{\pm},\rho^{\pm},b^{\pm}\|_{4,\pm})(|\psi|_{5}^{2}+|\psi|_{5}|\psi_{t}|_{4})\lesssim\sigma^{-1}P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)).

Now, it remains to control
I_{0}^{w}
and
I_{0}^{R}
. In view of Proposition
5.6
, it suffices to control the
H^{3.5}(\Sigma)
norms of
N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}
and
\Psi^{R}
. For the term
q_{w}^{\pm}
, we use trace lemma and div-curl inequality with tangential trace (see (
B.3
)) to get

\displaystyle|N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}|_{3.5}^{2}\leq|\psi|_{4.5}^{2}\|\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}\|_{4,\pm}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(|\psi|_{4.5})\left(\|\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}^{2}+\|\Delta^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm}^{2}+\left|N\times\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}\right|_{3.5}^{2}+\left|N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}\right|_{H^{3,5}(\Sigma^{\pm})}^{2}\right)

(5.63)

where the last three terms are zero because of

\nabla^{\varphi}\times\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}=\vec{0},\quad N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}|_{\Sigma^{\pm}}=0,\quad q_{w}^{\pm}|_{\Sigma}=0\Rightarrow N\times\nabla^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}|_{\Sigma}=(-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}q_{w}^{\pm},\overline{\partial}{}_{1}q_{w}^{\pm},\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\psi\overline{\partial}{}_{1}q_{w}^{\pm}-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\psi\overline{\partial}{}_{2}q_{w}^{+})^{\top}|_{\Sigma}=\vec{0}.

Then invoking the definition of
q_{w}^{\pm}
and using
\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\pm}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}
, we find

\displaystyle\|\Delta^{\varphi}q_{w}^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm}^{2}\leq C(|\psi|_{4},|\psi_{t}|_{3})\left(\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\mathcal{T}^{2}(b^{\pm},p^{\pm})\right\|_{3,\pm}^{2}+P\left(\left\|v^{\pm},b^{\pm}\right\|_{4,\pm}\right)\right)\leq P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t))\widetilde{E}_{5}(t).

(5.64)

Remark 5.2

(Necessity of anisotropic Sobolev spaces)
.

The term bounded by
\widetilde{E}_{5}(t)
is contributed exactly by the extra
\frac{1}{2}|b^{\pm}|^{2}
in the total pressure. From this, we can also see the necessity of the anisotropic Sobolev spaces when studying ideal compressible MHD. For Euler equations, the source terms for the wave equation only contain quadratic first-order terms, and one can use the trick in
[
32
,
62
]
to close the energy bound by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t)
. For incompressible MHD, the second-order time derivative term vanishes because
q^{\pm}
satisfies an elliptic equation.

The term
|\Psi^{R}|_{3.5}
can also be directly controlled. Recall that

\Psi^{R}:=\frac{\sigma}{2}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})(\mathcal{H}(\psi))-(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left(\left\llbracket\mathfrak{F}_{\psi}-\rho\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right\rrbracket\right)+\frac{\sigma}{2}\mathcal{R}_{\psi}^{\sigma}.

Using the Sobolev estimates for the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operators, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\sigma}{2}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})(\mathcal{H}(\psi))\right|_{3.5}+\left|(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}\left(\left\llbracket\mathfrak{F}_{\psi}-\rho\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right\rrbracket\right)\right|_{3.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~|\sigma\mathfrak{H}(\psi)|_{2.5}+\left|\mathfrak{F}_{\psi}^{\pm}\right|_{2.5}+\left|\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right|_{2.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\left((1+\sigma)|\psi|_{4.5}+|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}\right)\left(\left\|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}p^{\pm}\right\|_{2,\pm}+P(\|v^{\pm},b^{\pm}\|_{3,\pm},|\psi|_{3})\right)+\left|\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi\right|_{2.5}\lesssim P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t)).

(5.65)

Setting
s=4
in the remainder estimate (
5.42
), we have
|\sigma\mathcal{R}_{\psi}^{\sigma}|_{3.5}\leq C(|\psi|_{4.5})|\sigma\psi|_{5}\leq\sqrt{\sigma}C(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t))\sqrt{\mathcal{E}(t)}\leq\sqrt{\sigma}C(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t)).

Summarizing the estimates above, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}(t)\lesssim\sigma^{-1}P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t))E_{5}(t),

(5.66)

and under the stability condition (
1.27
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\mathcal{E}(t)+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(t))\lesssim P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t))\widetilde{E}_{5}(t).

(5.67)

Invoking Lemma
5.13
, we actually prove the following uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimate for fixed
\sigma>0

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi\right|_{5}^{2}+\left|\psi_{t}\right|_{3.5}^{2}\right)\lesssim\sigma^{-1}P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t))E_{5}(t),

(5.68)

and the following uniform-in-
({\varepsilon,\sigma})
estimate under the stability condition (
1.27
)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi\right|_{5}^{2}+\left|\psi\right|_{4.5}^{2}+\left|\psi_{t}\right|_{3.5}^{2}\right)\lesssim P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t))\widetilde{E}_{5}(t).

(5.69)

Step 3: Control of
|\psi_{tt}|_{2.5}
and
|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi_{t}|_{4}

It remains to prove the uniform estimates for
|\psi_{tt}|_{2.5}^{2}
. We just need to take one more
\partial_{t}
in the paralinearized evolution equation (
5.50
) to get

\displaystyle(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\partial_{t}^{3}\psi=

\displaystyle~-\frac{\sigma}{2}T_{\Lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}\partial_{t}\psi-(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})(\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j})\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}\psi-2(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}^{2}\psi

\displaystyle+\left(\rho^{+}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{i}^{+}\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{j}^{+}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{j})+\rho^{-}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{i}^{-}\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{j}^{-}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{j})\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}\psi

\displaystyle-(N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}\partial_{t}q_{w}^{+}+N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}\partial_{t}q_{w}^{-})+\underline{\partial_{t}\Psi^{R}}

\displaystyle+[\rho^{+}+\rho^{-},\partial_{t}]\partial_{t}^{2}\psi-\frac{\sigma}{2}[\partial_{t},T_{\Lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}]\psi-[\partial_{t},(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{j}]\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi-2[\partial_{t},(\rho^{+}+\rho^{-})\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{i}]\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}\psi

\displaystyle+\left[\partial_{t},\rho^{+}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{i}^{+}\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{j}^{+}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{j})+\rho^{-}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{i}^{-}\bar{\mathbf{b}}_{j}^{-}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{j})\right]\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\psi-[\partial_{t},N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}](q_{w}^{+}+q_{w}^{-}).

(5.70)

The proof follows in the same way as the above analysis for the
T_{\mathfrak{M}}T_{\mathfrak{n}}
-differentiated version of (
5.50
), so we skip most of the repeated details and only list the differences.

The first difference is that we should replace
T_{\mathfrak{M}}
by
T_{{\mathfrak{M}}^{\prime}}
where the symbol
{\mathfrak{M}}^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\frac{5}{2}}
is defined by

{\mathfrak{M}}^{\prime}:=(\mathfrak{m}^{(1.5)})^{\frac{5}{3}}=2^{\frac{5}{6}}|\xi|^{\frac{5}{2}}\left(1-\left|\frac{N}{|N|}\cdot\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{5}{4}}\in\Sigma^{\frac{5}{2}}.

The second difference is essential: we notice that the remainder term
\Psi^{R}
(defined in (
5.52
)) already contains second-order time derivative
(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)
. Taking one more time derivative, we know the underlined term
\partial_{t}\Psi^{R}
contains the following term

\partial_{t}\left((\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)\right)=(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{3}\psi)+[\partial_{t},(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}](\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{2}\psi)+(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\left\llbracket\partial_{t}\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{3}\psi),

and thus we are required to control
|(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{3}\psi)|_{2.5}
. Using Lemma
C.3
and Lemma
C.4
, we have

\left|(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1}(\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket\partial_{t}^{3}\psi)\right|_{2.5}\lesssim|\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket|_{1.5}|\partial_{t}^{3}\psi|_{1.5}.

Since we require
|\left\llbracket\rho\right\rrbracket|_{1.5}\lesssim\varepsilon
, we can control the right side by
|\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{3}\psi|_{1.5}

uniformly in
\varepsilon
. Under the stability condition (
1.27
), this term is already a part of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t)
. For fixed
\sigma>0
, we again invoke the kinematic boundary condition to get

|\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{3}\psi|_{1.5}\lesssim\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{2}v^{\pm}\|_{2,\pm}|\psi|_{2.5}+\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}v^{\pm}\|_{2,\pm}|\psi_{t}|_{2.5}+\|\varepsilon v^{\pm}\|_{2,\pm}|\psi_{tt}|_{2.5},

where the right side is already controlled by
P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t))
.

For
N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}\partial_{t}q_{w}^{\pm}
, we shall control the
H^{2.5}(\Sigma)
norm of them. Similarly as in Step 2 (the steps after the control of
I_{003}
), it suffices to obtain the bound for
\|\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}q^{\pm}\|_{2,\pm}
, but this is already a part of
E_{5}(t)
.

Finally, we note that the commutators between
\partial_{t}
and paradifferential operators arising from the right side of (
5.70
) can all be directly controlled by using the definition of paradifferential operators. For example, we have

\displaystyle\sigma[\partial_{t},T_{\Lambda}T_{\mathfrak{H}}]\psi=\sigma[\partial_{t},T_{\Lambda}]T_{\mathfrak{H}}\psi+\sigma T_{\Lambda}([\partial_{t},T_{\mathfrak{H}}]\psi)=\sigma T_{\partial_{t}\Lambda}(T_{\mathfrak{H}}\psi)+\sigma T_{\Lambda}T_{\partial_{t}\mathfrak{H}}\psi.

Then using the concrete form of
\Lambda
and
T_{\mathfrak{H}}
, we get

\displaystyle|\sigma T_{\partial_{t}\Lambda}(T_{\mathfrak{H}}\psi)|_{2.5}\lesssim|\partial_{t}\Lambda|_{L^{\infty}}|\sigma T_{\mathfrak{H}}\psi|_{2.5}\lesssim P(|\overline{\nabla}\psi,\overline{\nabla}\psi_{t}|_{L^{\infty}})|\sigma\psi|_{4.5}.

The term
\sigma T_{\Lambda}T_{\partial_{t}\mathfrak{H}}\psi
and other commutators can also be similarly controlled. Thus, we can conclude the following uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimate for fixed
\sigma>0

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi_{t}\right|_{4}^{2}+\left|\psi_{tt}\right|_{2.5}^{2}\right)\lesssim\sigma^{-1}P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t))E_{5}(t),

(5.71)

and the following uniform-in-
({\varepsilon,\sigma})
estimate under the stability condition (
1.27
)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\left|\sqrt{\sigma}\psi_{t}\right|_{4}^{2}+\left|\psi_{t}\right|_{3.5}^{2}+\left|\psi_{tt}\right|_{2.5}^{2}\right)\lesssim P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t))\widetilde{E}_{5}(t).

(5.72)

5.4
Improved double limits without the boundedness of
\geq 2
time derivatives

5.4.1
Improved incompressible limit for fixed
\sigma>0

From the analysis in Section
5.1
-Section
5.3
, we can prove the uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimates for
\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)
. For any
\delta\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)\lesssim\delta\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t)+P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(0))+P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},\mathfrak{E}_{4}(\tau))+E_{5}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(5.73)

For
1\leq l\leq 4
, since we do not change anything
\mathfrak{E}_{4+l}(t)
, we still have

\displaystyle l=1,2,3:

\displaystyle~~\mathfrak{E}_{4+l}(t)\lesssim\delta\mathfrak{E}_{4+l}(t)+P(\mathfrak{E}_{4+l}(0))+P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P\left(\sigma^{-1},\sum_{j=0}^{l}\mathfrak{E}_{4+j}(\tau)\right)+\mathfrak{E}_{4+l+1}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau;

(5.74)

\displaystyle l=4:

\displaystyle~~\mathfrak{E}_{8}(t)\lesssim\delta\mathfrak{E}_{8}(t)+P(\mathfrak{E}_{8}(0))+P(\mathfrak{E}_{4}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},\mathfrak{E}_{8}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(5.75)

Therefore, we get the Gronwall-type energy inequality for
\mathfrak{E}(t)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}(t)\lesssim\delta\mathfrak{E}(t)+P(\mathfrak{E}(0))+P(\mathfrak{E}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\sigma^{-1},\mathfrak{E}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(5.76)

Choosing
\delta>0
suitably small, the term
\delta\mathfrak{E}(t)
can be absorbed by the left side. Thus, there exists a time
T_{\sigma}^{\prime}>0
depending on
\sigma^{-1}
and the initial data, but independent of
\varepsilon
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\sigma}^{\prime}}\mathfrak{E}(t)\lesssim P(\sigma^{-1},\mathfrak{E}(0)).

(5.77)

With the uniform-in-
\varepsilon
estimates for
\mathfrak{E}(t)
, we now take the incompressible limit. Again, since
\|\partial_{t}(v,b)\|_{3}
is uniformly bounded with respect to
\varepsilon
and we still have
\varepsilon
-independent bound
|\psi_{t}|_{3.5}
, the Aubin-Lions compactness lemma gives the same strong convergence result as in Section
4.3
.

5.4.2
Improved double limits under the stability conditions

With the estimates (
5.69
) and (
5.49
) in Section
5.3
, we can get

|v_{t}\cdot N|_{2.5}^{2}+|b_{t}\cdot N|_{2.5}^{2}\lesssim P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(0))+P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(\tau))\widetilde{E}_{5}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

This finishes the control of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4}(t)
. Since
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}_{4+l}(t)=\widetilde{E}_{4+l}(t)
when
1\leq l\leq 4
and the strategies to control them remain unchanged, we can now close the energy estimates for
\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(t)
, uniformly in
\varepsilon
and
\sigma
, under the stability condition (
1.27
) (
d=3
) or (
1.35
) (
d=2
).

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(t)\leq P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(0))+P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(t))\int_{0}^{t}P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(\tau))\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

(5.78)

By Grönwall’s inequality, there exists
T^{\prime}>0
independent of
\sigma
and
\varepsilon
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(t)\leq P(\widetilde{\mathfrak{E}}(0)).

(5.79)

Thus, by Aubin-Lions compactness lemma, we can prove the same convergence result as in Theorem
1.2
.

Appendix A
Reynolds transport theorems

We record the Reynolds transport theorems used in this paper. For the proof, we refer to Luo-Zhang
[
36
, Appendix A]

Lemma A.1
.

Let
f,g
be smooth functions defined on
[0,T]\times\Omega
. Then:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\varphi}_{t}f)g\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}f(\widetilde{\varphi}_{t}g)\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x_{3}=0}fg\partial_{t}\psi\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(A.1)

Lemma A.2

(
Integration by parts for covariant derivatives
)
.

Let
f,g
be defined as in Lemma
A.1
. Then:

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}f)g\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x=-\int_{\Omega}f(\partial^{\varphi}_{i}g)\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{x_{3}=0}fgN_{i}\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}.

(A.3)

The following theorem holds.

Theorem A.3

(
Reynolds transport theorem
)
.

Let
f
be a smooth function defined on
[0,T]\times\Omega
. Then:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}\rho|f|^{2}\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\rho(D_{t}^{\varphi}f)f\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x.

(A.4)

Theorem
A.3
leads to the following corollary. The first one records the integration by parts formula for
D_{t}^{\varphi}
.

Corollary A.4

(
Reynolds transport theorem - a variant
)
.

It holds that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\Omega}fg\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}(D_{t}^{\varphi}f)g\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}f(D_{t}^{\varphi}g)\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}(\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot v)fg\partial_{3}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}x.

(A.5)

Appendix B
Preliminary lemmas about Sobolev inequalities

Lemma B.1

(Hodge-type elliptic estimates)
.

For any sufficiently smooth vector field
X
and
s\geq 1
, one has

\displaystyle\|X\|_{s}^{2}\leq C(|\psi|_{s},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|X\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot X\|_{s-1}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times X\|_{s-1}^{2}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{\alpha}X\|_{0}^{2}\right),

(B.1)

\displaystyle\|X\|_{s}^{2}\leq C^{\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|X\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot X\|_{s-1}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times X\|_{s-1}^{2}+|X\cdot N|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}^{2}\right),

(B.2)

\displaystyle\|X\|_{s}^{2}\leq C^{\prime\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|X\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot X\|_{s-1}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\times X\|_{s-1}^{2}+|X\times N|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}^{2}\right),

(B.3)

for any multi-index
\alpha
with
|\alpha|=s
. The constant
C(|\psi|_{s},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})>0
depends linearly on
|\psi|_{s}^{2}
and the constants
C^{\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})>0
and
C^{\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})>0
depend linearly on
|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}}^{2}
.

Lemma B.2

(Normal trace lemma)
.

For any sufficiently smooth vector field
X
and
s\geq 0
, one has

\displaystyle\left|X\cdot N\right|_{s-\frac{1}{2}}^{2}\lesssim C^{\prime\prime\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})\left(\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s}X\|_{0}^{2}+\|\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot X\|_{s-1}^{2}\right)

(B.4)

where the constant
C^{\prime\prime\prime}(|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}},|\overline{\nabla}\psi|_{W^{1,\infty}})>0
depends linearly on
|\psi|_{s+\frac{1}{2}}^{2}
.

We list two lemmas for the estimates of traces in the anisotropic Sobolev spaces. Define

L_{T}^{2}(H_{*}^{m}(\Omega^{\pm}))=\bigcap_{k=0}^{m}H^{k}((-\infty,T];H_{*}^{m-k}(\Omega^{\pm}))

with the norm
\|u\|_{m,*,T,\pm}:=\int_{-\infty}^{T}\|u(t)\|_{m,*,\pm}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t
. Similarly, we define

L_{T}^{2}(H^{m}(\Sigma))=\bigcap_{k=0}^{m}H^{k}((-\infty,T];H^{m-k}(\Sigma))

with the norm
|u|_{m,T}:=\int_{-\infty}^{T}|u(t)|_{m}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t
.

Lemma B.3

(Trace lemma for anisotropic Sobolev spaces)
.

Let
m\geq 1,~m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, then we have the following trace lemma for the anisotropic Sobolev space.

1.

If
f\in L_{T}^{2}(H_{*}^{m+1}(\Omega^{\pm}))
, then its trace
f|_{\Sigma}
belongs to
L_{T}^{2}(H^{m}(\Omega^{\pm}))
and satisfies

|f|_{m,T}\lesssim\|f\|_{m+1,*,T,\pm}.

2.

There exists a linear continuous operator
\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}:L_{T}^{2}(H^{m}(\Sigma))\to L_{T}^{2}(H_{*}^{m+1}(\Omega^{\pm}))
such that
(\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}g)|_{\Sigma}=g
and

\|\mathfrak{R}_{T}^{\pm}g\|_{m+1,*,T,\pm}\lesssim|g|_{m,T}.

Proof.

See
[
63
, Appendix B]
.
∎

There is one derivative loss in the above trace lemma, which is 1/2-order more than the trace lemma for standard Sobolev spaces. Indeed, for
\Omega^{\pm}
defined in this paper, we have the following estimate that will be applied to control the non-characteristic variables
q,v\cdot\mathbf{N}
and
b\cdot\mathbf{N}
.

Lemma B.4

(An estimate for traces of non-characteristic variables)
.

Let
\Omega^{\pm}:=\mathbb{T}^{d-1}\times\{0\lessgtr x_{d}\lessgtr\pm H\}
,
\Sigma=\mathbb{T}^{d-1}\times\{x_{d}=0\}
and
\Sigma^{\pm}=\mathbb{T}^{d-1}\times\{\pm H\}
. Let
\mathcal{T}^{\alpha}=(\omega(x_{d})\partial_{d})^{\alpha_{d+1}}\partial_{t}^{\alpha_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\cdots\overline{\partial}{}_{d-1}^{\alpha_{d-1}}\partial_{d}^{\alpha_{d}}
with
\langle\alpha\rangle:=\alpha_{0}+\cdots+\alpha_{d-1}+2\alpha_{d}+\alpha_{d+1}=m-1,~m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. Let
q^{\pm}(t,x)\in H_{*}^{m}(\Omega)
satisfy
\|q^{\pm}(t)\|_{m,*,\pm}+\|\partial_{d}q^{\pm}(t)\|_{m-1,*,\pm}<\infty
for any
0\leq t\leq T
and let
f^{\pm}\in H_{*}^{2}(\Omega^{\pm})\cap H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega^{\pm})
be a function vanishing on
\Sigma^{\pm}
. Then we have

\int_{\Sigma}(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}q^{\pm})\,(\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle f^{\pm})\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\leq(\|\partial_{d}q^{\pm}\|_{m-1,*,\pm}+\|q^{\pm}\|_{m,*,\pm})\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}f^{\pm}\|_{1,\pm}

(B.5)

In particular, for
s\geq 1
, we have the following inequality for any
g^{\pm}\in H_{*}^{s}(\Omega^{\pm})
with
g^{\pm}|_{\Sigma^{\pm}}=0
.

|g^{\pm}|_{s-1/2}^{2}\leq\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s}g^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}\|\langle\overline{\partial}{}\rangle^{s-1}\partial_{d}g^{\pm}\|_{0,\pm}\leq\|g^{\pm}\|_{s,*,\pm}\|\partial_{d}g^{\pm}\|_{s-1,*,\pm}.

Proof.

See
[
63
, Appendix B]
.
∎

The following lemma concerns the Sobolev embeddings.

Lemma B.5
.

We have the following inequalities

\displaystyle H^{m}(\Omega^{\pm})\hookrightarrow H_{*}^{m}(\Omega^{\pm})\hookrightarrow

\displaystyle H^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}(\Omega^{\pm}),~~\forall m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})}\lesssim\|u\|_{H_{*}^{3}(\Omega^{\pm})},~~

\displaystyle\|u\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})}\lesssim\|u\|_{H_{*}^{5}(\Omega^{\pm})},~~|u|_{W^{1,\infty}(\Omega^{\pm})}\lesssim\|u\|_{H_{*}^{5}(\Omega^{\pm})}.

We also need the following Kato-Ponce type multiplicative Sobolev inequality.

Lemma B.6

(
[
27
]
)
.

Let
J=(1-\Delta)^{1/2}
,
s\geq 0
. Then the following estimates hold:

\|J^{s}(fg)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{s,p_{1}}}\|g\|_{L^{p_{2}}}+\|f\|_{L^{q_{1}}}\|g\|_{W^{s,q_{2}}},

(B.6)

where
1/2=1/p_{1}+1/p_{2}=1/q_{1}+1/q_{2}
and
2\leq p_{1},q_{2}<\infty
.

\|[J^{s},f]g\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\|\partial f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|J^{s-1}g\|_{L^{p}}+\|J^{s}f\|_{L^{p}}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}}

(B.7)

where
s\geq 0
and
1<p<\infty
.

We also need the following transport-type estimate in order to close the uniform estimates for the nonlinear approximate system.

Lemma B.7
.

Let
f(t)\in W^{1,1}(0,T)
and
g\in L^{1}(0,T)
and
\kappa>0
. Assume that

f(t)+\kappa f^{\prime}(t)\leq g(t)~~\text{a.e. }t\in(0,T).

Then for any
t\in(0,T)
,

\sup_{\tau\in[0,t]}f(\tau)\leq f(0)+\mathop{\mathrm{ess~sup}}\limits_{\tau\in(0,t)}|g(\tau)|.

Appendix C
Paraproducts and the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator

C.1
Bony’s paraproduct decomposition

We already introduce the paradifferential operator in Section
5.2.2
. Here we present the relations between paradifferential operators and paraproducts. The cutoff function
\tilde{\chi}(\xi,\eta)
in the definition of
T_{a}u
is

\tilde{\chi}(\xi,\eta)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Theta_{k-3}(\xi)\vartheta(\eta),

where
\Theta(\xi)=1
when
|\xi|\leq 1
and
\Theta(\xi)=0
when
|\xi|\geq 2
and

\Theta_{k}(\xi):=\Theta(\frac{\xi}{2}),~~k\in\mathbb{Z},\quad\vartheta_{0}=\Theta,\quad\vartheta_{k}:=\Theta_{k}-\Theta_{k-1},~~k\geq 1.

Based on this, we can introduce the Littlewood-Paley projections
\mathcal{P}_{k}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\leq k}
as follows

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{P}_{k}u}(\xi):=\vartheta_{k}(\xi)\hat{u}(\xi),~~\forall k\geq 0,\quad\mathcal{P}_{k}u:=0~~\forall k<0,\quad\mathcal{P}_{\leq k}u:=\sum_{l\leq k}\mathcal{P}_{l}u.

When the symbol
a(x,\xi)
(in the paradifferential operator
T_{a}
) does not depend on
\xi
, we can take
\psi(\eta)\equiv 1
and then we have

T_{a}u=\sum_{k}\mathcal{P}_{\leq k-3}a(\mathcal{P}_{k}u)

which is the usual Bony’s paraproduct. In general, the well-known Bony’s paraproduct decomposition is

au=T_{a}u+T_{u}a+R(u,a),\quad R(u,a)=\sum_{|k-l|\leq 2}(\mathcal{P}_{k}a)(\mathcal{P}_{l}u).

We have the following estimates for the remainder
R(u,a)

Lemma C.1

(
[
3
, Section 2.3]
)
.

For
s\in\mathbb{R},~r<d/2,~\delta>0
, we have

|T_{a}u|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\min\{|a|_{L^{\infty}}|u|_{H^{s}},|a|_{H^{r}}|u|_{H^{s+\frac{d}{2}-r}},|a|_{H^{\frac{d}{2}}}|u|_{H^{s+\delta}}\}

and for any
s>0,s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
s_{1}+s_{2}=s+\frac{d}{2}
, we have

|R(u,a)|_{H^{s}}\lesssim|a|_{H^{s_{1}}}|u|_{H^{s_{2}}}.

C.2
Basic properties of the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator

Let the space dimension
d=3
for simplicity. Given a function
f:\Sigma=\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
, we define the Dirichlet-to-Neumann (DtN) operator (with respect to
\psi
and region
\Omega^{\pm}
) by

\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}f:=\mp N\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}(\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f)|_{\Sigma},\quad-\Delta^{\varphi}(\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f)=0\text{ in }\Omega^{\pm},~~\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f|_{\Sigma}=f,~~\partial_{3}(\mathcal{E}_{\psi}^{\pm}f)|_{\Sigma^{\pm}}=0.

Here the Laplacian operator is defined by
\Delta^{\varphi}:=\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}=\partial_{i}(\bm{E}^{ij}\partial_{j})
with

\bm{E}=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{3}\varphi&0&-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\varphi\\
0&\partial_{3}\varphi&-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\varphi\\
-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\varphi&-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\varphi&\frac{1+|\overline{\nabla}\varphi|^{2}}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{\partial_{3}\varphi}\bm{P}\bm{P}^{\top},~~\bm{P}:=\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{3}\varphi&0&0\\
0&\partial_{3}\varphi&0\\
-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\varphi&-\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\varphi&1\end{bmatrix},

and
\varphi(t,x):=x_{3}+\chi(x_{3})\psi(t,x^{\prime})
is defined in (
1.4
) as the extension of
\psi
into
\Omega^{\pm}
. The choice of
\chi(x_{3})
is slightly different from
[
2
,
3
,
4
]
, but it does not introduce any substantial difference because the expression of
\Delta^{\varphi}
is still written to be
\Delta^{\varphi}:=\nabla^{\varphi}\cdot\nabla^{\varphi}=\partial_{i}(\bm{E}^{ij}\partial_{j})
and we have
\Delta^{\varphi}\varphi=0
in
\Omega^{\pm}
. The DtN operators satisfy the following estimates and we refer to
[
48
, Appendix A.4]
for the proof.

Lemma C.2

(Sobolev estimates for DtN operators)
.

For
s>2+\frac{d}{2},~-\frac{1}{2}\leq r\leq s-1
and
\psi\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, we have

|\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}f|_{r}\leq C(|\psi|_{s})|f|_{r+1}.

Lemma C.3

(Remainder estimates for DtN operators)
.

For
s>2+\frac{d}{2}
and
\psi\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, we have

\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm}f=T_{\Lambda^{(1),\pm}}f+R_{1}^{\pm}(f)

with
\Lambda^{(1),+}=\Lambda^{(1),\pm}
defined in Proposition
5.7
and

\forall r\in[\frac{1}{2},s-1],\quad|R_{1}^{\pm}(f)|_{r}+|(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{+}-\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{-})f|_{r}\leq C(|\psi|_{s})|f|_{r}.

Lemma C.4

(Sobolev estimates for the inverse of DtN operators)
.

For
s>2+\frac{d}{2},~-\frac{1}{2}\leq r\leq s-1
and
\psi\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, we have

|(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}^{\pm})^{-1}f|_{r+1}\leq C(|\psi|_{s})|f|_{r}.
